        pub fn subject(&self) -> &str {
            &self.subject
        }
    
        /// The paths the commit touched, in the order `git` listed them; never
        /// empty in a document log, and empty in the logs that list none.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn paths(&self) -> &[String] {
            &self.paths
        }
    }
    
    /// Run a log query: an output past the bound is [`GitError::LogTooLarge`].
    fn log_query(runner: &dyn Runner, invocation: &Invocation) -> Result<Output, GitError> {
        let limits = Limits::query();
        match runner.run(invocation, &limits) {
            Ok(output) => Ok(output),
            Err(ExecError::OutputTooLarge {
                stream: Stream::Stdout,
                limit,
                ..
            }) => Err(GitError::LogTooLarge {
                command: invocation.to_string(),
                limit,
            }),
            Err(error) => Err(error.into()),
        }
    }
    
    /// The error of an output that is not what the log allows.
    fn malformed(invocation: &Invocation, expected: &'static str, line: &[u8]) -> GitError {
        GitError::Answer {
            command: invocation.to_string(),
            expected,
            answer: shown(&String::from_utf8_lossy(line)),
        }
    }
    
    /// The header of one record: `<commit> 0x1f <date> 0x1f <subject>`.
    fn header(invocation: &Invocation, line: &[u8]) -> Result<LogEntry, GitError> {
        const EXPECTED: &str = "a record of a commit id, an author date and a subject";
        let mut fields = line.splitn(3, |byte| *byte == UNIT);
        let (Some(commit), Some(date), Some(subject)) = (fields.next(), fields.next(), fields.next())
        else {
            return Err(malformed(invocation, EXPECTED, line));
        };
        let commit = std::str::from_utf8(commit)
            .ok()
            .and_then(|text| Commit::new(text).ok())
            .ok_or_else(|| malformed(invocation, "a commit id", line))?;
        let date = std::str::from_utf8(date)
            .ok()
            .and_then(AuthorDate::parse)
            .ok_or_else(|| malformed(invocation, "a strict ISO 8601 author date", line))?;
        Ok(LogEntry {
            commit,
            date,
            subject: String::from_utf8_lossy(subject).into_owned(),
            paths: Vec::new(),
        })
    }
    
    /// A path line of `--name-only`, unquoted when `git` quoted it.
    fn path_line(invocation: &Invocation, line: &[u8]) -> Result<String, GitError> {
        let bytes = match line {
            [b'"', inner @ .., b'"'] => {
                unquote(inner).ok_or_else(|| malformed(invocation, "a quoted path", line))?
            }
            _ => line.to_vec(),
        };
        String::from_utf8(bytes).map_err(|error| GitError::NotUtf8 {
            reason: format!("`{invocation}` listed a path that is not UTF-8: {error}"),
        })
    }
    
    /// The bytes of a C-quoted name without its quotes, as `git` writes them:
    /// `\a \b \t \n \v \f \r \" \\` and three octal digits for any other byte.
    fn unquote(inner: &[u8]) -> Option<Vec<u8>> {
        let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(inner.len());
        let mut at = 0;
        while let Some(byte) = inner.get(at).copied() {
            at += 1;
            if byte == b'"' {
                return None;
            }
            if byte != b'\\' {
                out.push(byte);
                continue;
            }
            let escape = inner.get(at).copied()?;
            at += 1;
            out.push(match escape {
                b'a' => 0x07,
                b'b' => 0x08,
                b't' => b'\t',
                b'n' => b'\n',
                b'v' => 0x0b,
                b'f' => 0x0c,
                b'r' => b'\r',
                b'"' => b'"',
                b'\\' => b'\\',
                b'0'..=b'3' => {
                    let rest = inner.get(at..at + 2)?;
                    if !rest.iter().all(|b| (b'0'..=b'7').contains(b)) {
                        return None;
                    }
                    at += 2;
                    (escape - b'0') * 64 + (rest[0] - b'0') * 8 + (rest[1] - b'0')
                }
                _ => return None,
            });
        }
        Some(out)
    }
    
    /// The records of a history log. With `paths` the lines after a header are
    /// the paths of its commit; without, a line that is not a header and not
    /// empty is an error.
    fn records(
        invocation: &Invocation,
        output: &Output,
        paths: bool,
    ) -> Result<Vec<LogEntry>, GitError> {
        let mut entries: Vec<LogEntry> = Vec::new();
        let text = output.stdout.strip_suffix(b"\n").unwrap_or(&output.stdout);
        if text.is_empty() {
            return Ok(entries);
        }
        for line in text.split(|byte| *byte == b'\n') {
            if let Some(rest) = line.strip_prefix(&[RECORD]) {
                entries.push(header(invocation, rest)?);
            } else if line.is_empty() {
            } else if let (true, Some(entry)) = (paths, entries.last_mut()) {
                entry.paths.push(path_line(invocation, line)?);
            } else {
                return Err(malformed(
                    invocation,
                    "a record that begins with 0x1e",
                    line,
                ));
            }
        }
        Ok(entries)
    }
    
    /// Whether the repository is a shallow clone, whose history stops at a
    /// boundary: `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository`.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// [`GitError::Failed`] when the command fails; [`GitError::Answer`] when the
    /// answer is not `true` or `false`; [`GitError::Exec`] when it cannot run.
    pub fn is_shallow(runner: &dyn Runner) -> Result<bool, GitError> {
        let invocation = Invocation::git_shallow();
        let output = runner.run(&invocation, &Limits::query())?;
        if !output.success() {
            return Err(failed(&invocation, &output));
        }
        match one_line(&invocation, &output, "`true` or `false`")? {
            "true" => Ok(true),
            "false" => Ok(false),
            other => Err(GitError::Answer {
                command: invocation.to_string(),
                expected: "`true` or `false`",
                answer: shown(other),
            }),
        }
    }
    
    /// The commits of a file, newest first as `git` lists them, through the
    /// moves `git` detects, each with the name the file had at that commit:
    /// `git --no-pager log --no-show-signature --follow --cc --root
    /// --format=%x1e%H%x1f%aI%x1f%s --name-only -- <doc-path>`. The history asks
    /// it of a document and of each file of a bound module. `--cc` lists a merge
    /// whose resolution changed the file (a merge that took one side unchanged is
    /// not listed, as it changed nothing), and `--root` keeps a user's
    /// `log.showRoot=false` from hiding the commit that created it. A commit that
    /// lists no path, as `--follow` lists a merge that changed nothing in the
    /// file, is left out. A file that no commit holds has an empty log. The whole
    /// log is read: no date limits it.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// [`GitError::LogTooLarge`] for an output over 16 MiB; [`GitError::Answer`]
    /// or [`GitError::NotUtf8`] for an output that is not a log of this form;
    /// [`GitError::Failed`] when the command fails; [`GitError::Exec`] when it
    /// cannot run.
    pub fn document_log(runner: &dyn Runner, path: &TreePath) -> Result<Vec<LogEntry>, GitError> {
        let invocation = Invocation::git_document_log(path);
        let output = log_query(runner, &invocation)?;
        if !output.success() {
            return Err(failed(&invocation, &output));
        }
        let mut entries = records(&invocation, &output, true)?;
        // `--follow` lists a merge that changed nothing in the document, with no
        // path; it did not shape the document and is not an entry.
        entries.retain(|entry| !entry.paths.is_empty());
        Ok(entries)
    }
    
    /// The commits that changed the lines of a file, newest first, traced back
    /// from `HEAD`: `git --no-pager log --no-show-signature -L<start>,<end>:<file>
    /// --no-patch --format=%x1e%H%x1f%aI%x1f%s --`. The entries list no path. The
    /// whole log is read: no date limits it.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// As [`document_log`]; a file that is not in `HEAD`, or whose copy there ends
    /// before the first line of `lines`, is [`GitError::Failed`].
    pub fn symbol_log(
        runner: &dyn Runner,
        lines: LineRange,
        file: &TreePath,
    ) -> Result<Vec<LogEntry>, GitError> {
        let invocation = Invocation::git_symbol_log(lines, file);
        let output = log_query(runner, &invocation)?;
        if !output.success() {
            return Err(failed(&invocation, &output));
        }
        records(&invocation, &output, false)
    }
    
    /// The newest commit that changed how often an identifier occurs in a crate,
    /// as [`last_change`] answers it.
    #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    pub struct LastChange {
        entry: LogEntry,
        parent: Option<Commit>,
    }
    
    impl LastChange {
        /// The commit, its date and its subject; it lists no path.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn entry(&self) -> &LogEntry {
            &self.entry
        }
    
        /// Its first parent, or `None` for the first commit of the history.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn parent(&self) -> Option<&Commit> {
            self.parent.as_ref()
        }
    }
    
    /// The newest commit that changed how often `identifier` occurs in the files
    /// below `dirs`, with its first parent, or `None` when no commit did: `git
    /// --no-pager log --no-show-signature --root -S<identifier> -n 1
    /// --format=%H%x1f%P%x1f%aI%x1f%s -- <crate-dir>...`. `--root` keeps a
    /// user's `log.showRoot=false` from hiding the first commit. The parents are
    /// the ids `%P` prints, each accepted only by the commit-id pattern; none for
    /// the first commit of the history.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// As [`document_log`].
    pub fn last_change(
        runner: &dyn Runner,
        identifier: &Identifier,
        dirs: &[TreePath],
    ) -> Result<Option<LastChange>, GitError> {
        const EXPECTED: &str = "a commit id, its parents, an author date and a subject";
        let invocation = Invocation::git_pickaxe(identifier, dirs);
        let output = log_query(runner, &invocation)?;
        if !output.success() {
            return Err(failed(&invocation, &output));
        }
        let text = output.stdout.strip_suffix(b"\n").unwrap_or(&output.stdout);
        if text.is_empty() {
            return Ok(None);
        }
        if text.contains(&b'\n') {
            return Err(malformed(&invocation, "one record", text));
        }
        let mut fields = text.splitn(3, |byte| *byte == UNIT);
        let (Some(commit), Some(parents), Some(rest)) = (fields.next(), fields.next(), fields.next())
        else {
            return Err(malformed(&invocation, EXPECTED, text));
        };
        let parents = std::str::from_utf8(parents)
            .ok()
            .and_then(|list| {
                if list.is_empty() {
                    Some(Vec::new())
                } else {
                    list.split(' ')
                        .map(|id| Commit::new(id).ok())
                        .collect::<Option<Vec<Commit>>>()
                }
            })
            .ok_or_else(|| malformed(&invocation, "the parent ids of a commit", text))?;
        let mut line = commit.to_vec();
        line.push(UNIT);
        line.extend_from_slice(rest);
        Ok(Some(LastChange {
            entry: header(&invocation, &line)?,
            parent: parents.into_iter().next(),
        }))
    }
    
    #[cfg(test)]
    mod tests {
        use super::*;
        use crate::exec::{FakeRunner, Program};
    
        const A: &str = "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef01234567";
        const B: &str = "fedcba9876543210fedcba9876543210fedcba98";
    
        #[allow(
            clippy::unnecessary_wraps,
            reason = "a scripted answer of the fake is a result"
        )]
        fn ok(stdout: &[u8]) -> Result<Output, ExecError> {
            Ok(Output::new(Some(0), stdout.to_vec(), Vec::new()))
        }
    
        fn path(text: &str) -> TreePath {
            TreePath::new(text).unwrap()
        }
    
        fn doc_argv(path: &str) -> Vec<String> {
            Invocation::git_document_log(&TreePath::new(path).unwrap()).argv()
        }
    
        fn run_doc(stdout: &[u8]) -> Result<Vec<LogEntry>, GitError> {
            let owned = doc_argv("docs/a.md");
            let args: Vec<&str> = owned.iter().map(String::as_str).collect();
            let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &args, ok(stdout));
            document_log(&runner, &path("docs/a.md"))
        }
    
        #[test]
        fn a_strict_author_date_is_read_and_ordered_by_its_instant() {
            let utc = AuthorDate::parse("2026-05-02T10:00:00Z").unwrap();
            let east = AuthorDate::parse("2026-05-02T12:00:00+02:00").unwrap();
            let west = AuthorDate::parse("2026-05-02T05:00:01-05:00").unwrap();
            assert_eq!(utc.seconds(), east.seconds());
            assert!(west > utc && west > east);
            assert_eq!(east.day(), "2026-05-02");
            assert_eq!(
                AuthorDate::parse("1970-01-01T00:00:00+00:00")
                    .unwrap()
                    .seconds(),
                0
            );
            assert_eq!(
                AuthorDate::parse("2000-03-01T00:00:00Z").unwrap().seconds(),
                951_868_800
            );
            assert!(AuthorDate::parse("2028-02-29T00:00:00Z").is_some());
            assert!(AuthorDate::parse("2016-12-31T23:59:59Z").is_some());
            assert!(AuthorDate::parse("2017-01-01T00:59:59+01:00").is_some());
            for refused in [
                "",
                "2026-05-02",
                "2026-05-02 10:00:00 +0200",
                "2026-13-01T00:00:00Z",
                "2026-02-29T00:00:00Z",
                "2026-05-02T24:00:00Z",
                "2026-05-02T10:00:00+0200",
                "2026-05-02T10:00:00",
                "2026-05-02T10:00:00+02:00x",
                "2026-05-0xT10:00:00Z",
                "+026-05-02T10:00:00Z",
                "2016-12-31T23:59:60Z",
                "2017-01-01T00:59:60+01:00",
                "2026-05-02T10:00:60Z",
                "2026-05-02T10:00:00.0Z",
                "2026-05-02t10:00:00Z",
                "2026-05-02T10:00:00z",
            ] {
                assert!(AuthorDate::parse(refused).is_none(), "{refused:?}");
            }
        }
    
        #[test]
        fn a_since_day_starts_at_midnight_utc_on_the_scale_of_an_author_date() {
            let day = |text: &str| start_of_day(&Date::new(text).unwrap());
            assert_eq!(day("1970-01-01"), 0);
            assert_eq!(day("2000-03-01"), 951_868_800);
            for (since, date) in [
                ("2026-10-15", "2026-10-15T00:00:00Z"),
                ("2026-10-15", "2026-10-15T02:00:00+02:00"),
                ("2028-02-29", "2028-02-28T19:00:00-05:00"),
                ("2099-12-31", "2099-12-31T00:00:00+00:00"),
            ] {
                assert_eq!(
                    day(since),
                    AuthorDate::parse(date).unwrap().seconds(),
                    "{since} {date}"
                );
            }
            // The neighbours: one second before the day is before it.
            let before = AuthorDate::parse("2026-10-14T23:59:59Z").unwrap();
            assert_eq!(before.seconds() + 1, day("2026-10-15"));
        }
    
        #[test]
        fn a_document_log_reads_each_record_with_its_paths() {
            let stdout = format!(
                "\x1e{A}\x1f2026-05-02T10:00:00+00:00\x1fsecond\n\ndocs/a.md\n\
                 \x1e{B}\x1f2026-05-01T10:00:00+00:00\x1fa merge\n\
                 \x1e{A}\x1f2026-04-01T10:00:00+00:00\x1ffirst\n\ndocs/old.md\n\"docs/\\303\\251\\t.md\"\n"
            );
            let entries = run_doc(stdout.as_bytes()).unwrap();
            // The merge that lists no path changed nothing in the document.
            assert_eq!(entries.len(), 2);
            assert_eq!(entries[0].subject(), "second");
            assert_eq!(entries[0].paths(), ["docs/a.md"]);
            assert_eq!(entries[1].subject(), "first");
            assert_eq!(entries[1].paths(), ["docs/old.md", "docs/\u{e9}\t.md"]);
            assert_eq!(run_doc(b"").unwrap(), []);
        }
    
        #[test]
        fn a_subject_keeps_every_byte_but_a_newline() {
            let subject = format!("a\x1fb\x1e{A}\x1f2026-01-01T00:00:00Z\x1f| x \u{1b}[31m");
            let stdout = format!("\x1e{A}\x1f2026-05-02T10:00:00Z\x1f{subject}\n\ndocs/a.md\n");
            let entries = run_doc(stdout.as_bytes()).unwrap();
            assert_eq!(entries.len(), 1);
            assert_eq!(entries[0].subject(), subject);
            assert_eq!(entries[0].commit().as_str(), A);
            // Invalid UTF-8 is shown as U+FFFD, the rest kept.
            let mut bytes = format!("\x1e{A}\x1f2026-05-02T10:00:00Z\x1fbad ").into_bytes();
            bytes.extend([0xff, b'!', b'\n']);
            bytes.extend(b"\ndocs/a.md\n");
            assert_eq!(run_doc(&bytes).unwrap()[0].subject(), "bad \u{fffd}!");
        }
    
        #[test]
        fn output_that_is_not_a_log_of_the_form_is_an_error() {
            for bad in [
                "docs/a.md\n".to_string(),
                "garbage before\n\x1e".to_string(),
                format!("\x1e{A}\x1f2026-05-02T10:00:00Z\n"),
                "\x1eHEAD\x1f2026-05-02T10:00:00Z\x1fs\n".to_string(),
                format!("\x1e{A}\x1fyesterday\x1fs\n"),
                format!("\x1e{A}\x1f2026-05-02T10:00:00Z\x1fs\n\n\"unterminated\\\"\n"),
                format!("\x1e{A}\x1f2026-05-02T10:00:00Z\x1fs\n\n\"bad \\q\"\n"),
            ] {
                let error = run_doc(bad.as_bytes()).expect_err(&bad);
                assert!(
                    matches!(error, GitError::Answer { .. }),
                    "{bad:?}: {error:?}"
                );
            }
            let mut bytes = format!("\x1e{A}\x1f2026-05-02T10:00:00Z\x1fs\n\n").into_bytes();
            bytes.extend([b'a', 0xff, b'\n']);
            assert!(matches!(run_doc(&bytes), Err(GitError::NotUtf8 { .. })));
        }
    
        #[test]
        fn a_symbol_log_lists_no_path_and_refuses_any_other_line() {
            let lines = LineRange::new(3, 9).unwrap();
            let owned = Invocation::git_symbol_log(lines, &path("src/lib.rs")).argv();
            let args: Vec<&str> = owned.iter().map(String::as_str).collect();
            let good = format!("\x1e{A}\x1f2026-05-02T10:00:00Z\x1fs\n");
            let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &args, ok(good.as_bytes()));
            let entries = symbol_log(&runner, lines, &path("src/lib.rs")).unwrap();
            assert_eq!(entries.len(), 1);
            assert_eq!(entries[0].paths(), [] as [String; 0]);
            // A patch printed despite `--no-patch` is not read as a path.
            let patch = format!("{good}\ndiff --git a/src/lib.rs b/src/lib.rs\n");
            let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &args, ok(patch.as_bytes()));
            assert!(matches!(
                symbol_log(&runner, lines, &path("src/lib.rs")),
                Err(GitError::Answer { .. })
            ));
        }
    
        #[test]
        fn a_log_past_the_bound_is_an_error_that_says_so() {
            let owned = doc_argv("docs/a.md");
            let args: Vec<&str> = owned.iter().map(String::as_str).collect();
            let big = vec![b'a'; crate::exec::DEFAULT_OUTPUT_BOUND + 1];
            let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &args, ok(&big));
            let error = document_log(&runner, &path("docs/a.md")).expect_err("too large");
            assert!(
                matches!(&error, GitError::LogTooLarge { limit, .. } if *limit == crate::exec::DEFAULT_OUTPUT_BOUND),
                "{error:?}"
            );
            assert!(error.to_string().contains("16777216"), "{error}");
            // The neighbour: exactly the bound is read.
            let mut exact = format!("\x1e{A}\x1f2026-05-02T10:00:00Z\x1f").into_bytes();
            let tail = b"\n\ndocs/a.md\n";
            exact.resize(crate::exec::DEFAULT_OUTPUT_BOUND - tail.len(), b's');
            exact.extend(tail);
            let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &args, ok(&exact));
            assert_eq!(document_log(&runner, &path("docs/a.md")).unwrap().len(), 1);
        }
    
        #[test]
        fn the_pickaxe_answers_one_record_with_its_parent_or_none() {
            let id = Identifier::new("gone").unwrap();
            let dirs = [path("crates/a")];
            let owned = Invocation::git_pickaxe(&id, &dirs).argv();
            let args: Vec<&str> = owned.iter().map(String::as_str).collect();
            let answer = |stdout: &str| {
                let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &args, ok(stdout.as_bytes()));
                last_change(&runner, &id, &dirs)
            };
            let one = format!("{A}\x1f{B}\x1f2026-05-02T10:00:00Z\x1fremove gone\x1f!\n");
            let found = answer(&one).unwrap().unwrap();
            assert_eq!(found.entry().commit().as_str(), A);
            assert_eq!(found.entry().subject(), "remove gone\x1f!");
            assert_eq!(found.parent().map(Commit::as_str), Some(B));
            // A merge: the first parent is the one reported.
            let merge = answer(&format!("{A}\x1f{B} {A}\x1f2026-05-02T10:00:00Z\x1fm\n"))
                .unwrap()
                .unwrap();
            assert_eq!(merge.parent().map(Commit::as_str), Some(B));
            // The first commit of the history has no parent.
            let root = answer(&format!("{A}\x1f\x1f2026-05-02T10:00:00Z\x1froot\n"))
                .unwrap()
                .unwrap();
            assert_eq!(root.parent(), None);
            assert_eq!(answer("").unwrap(), None);
            for bad in [
                format!("{one}{one}"),
                format!("{A}\x1fHEAD\x1f2026-05-02T10:00:00Z\x1fs\n"),
                format!("{A}\x1f{B}  {A}\x1f2026-05-02T10:00:00Z\x1fs\n"),
                format!("{A}\x1f{B}\n"),
                format!("{A}\x1f{B}\x1fnot a date\x1fs\n"),
            ] {
                assert!(
                    matches!(answer(&bad), Err(GitError::Answer { .. })),
                    "{bad:?}"
                );
            }
        }
    
        #[test]
        fn the_shallow_answer_is_true_or_false() {
            for (answer, expected) in [
                ("true\n", Some(true)),
                ("false\n", Some(false)),
                ("yes\n", None),
            ] {
                let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(
                    Program::Git,
                    &["rev-parse", "--is-shallow-repository"],
                    ok(answer.as_bytes()),
                );
                assert_eq!(is_shallow(&runner).ok(), expected, "{answer}");
            }
        }
    }

    // SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2026 Blackcat Informatics® Inc. <paudley@blackcatinformatics.ca>

    //! The git questions against scripted answers: the version text, the exact argv
    //! of each question, the shape of each answer and every refusal. The real
    //! `git` is exercised by `tests/git.rs`.
    
    use raf_okf::MAX_TEXT_BYTES;
    
    use super::{
        GitError, GitVersion, MergeBase, blob_at, default_branch_ref, default_merge_base, merge_base,
        require_version, resolve_commit, show_prefix, tracked_paths,
    };
    use crate::exec::{
        Call, Commit, ExecError, FakeRunner, Limits, Output, Program, RefName, SourcePath, Stream,
    };
    
    const COMMIT: &str = "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef01234567";
    const OTHER: &str = "fedcba9876543210fedcba9876543210fedcba98";
    
    fn said(text: &str) -> Output {
        Output::new(Some(0), text.as_bytes().to_vec(), Vec::new())
    }
    
    fn failed(code: i32, stderr: &str) -> Output {
        Output::new(Some(code), Vec::new(), stderr.as_bytes().to_vec())
    }
    
    fn commit(text: &str) -> Commit {
        Commit::new(text).expect("a commit id")
    }
    
    fn reference(text: &str) -> RefName {
        RefName::new(text).expect("a reference name")
    }
    
    fn path(text: &str) -> SourcePath {
        SourcePath::new(text).expect("a source path")
    }
    
    // ------------------------------------------------------------------ version
    
    #[test]
    fn a_version_is_read_from_the_text_git_prints() {
        let table = [
            ("git version 2.45.1\n", (2, 45, 1)),
            ("git version 2.45.1", (2, 45, 1)),
            ("git version 2.45.1.windows.1\n", (2, 45, 1)),
            ("git version 2.39.5 (Apple Git-154)\n", (2, 39, 5)),
            ("git version 2.30.0\n", (2, 30, 0)),
            ("git version 2.30\n", (2, 30, 0)),
            ("git version 2.46.0-rc1\n", (2, 46, 0)),
            ("git version 2.47.0.vfs.0.1\n", (2, 47, 0)),
            ("git version 3.0.0\nsecond line\n", (3, 0, 0)),
            ("git version 0002.00030.0001", (2, 30, 1)),
            ("git version 2.30.rc1", (2, 30, 0)),
            ("git version 2.30.4294967295", (2, 30, u32::MAX)),
        ];
        for (text, (major, minor, patch)) in table {
            let version = GitVersion::parse(text).unwrap_or_else(|error| panic!("{text:?}: {error}"));
            assert_eq!(
                (version.major(), version.minor(), version.patch()),
                (major, minor, patch),
                "{text:?}"
            );
            assert_eq!(version.to_string(), format!("{major}.{minor}.{patch}"));
        }
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn a_text_that_is_not_a_version_is_refused() {
        for text in [
            "",
            "\n",
            "git",
            "git version",
            "git version ",
            "git version x.y",
            "git version 2",
            "git version 2.",
            "git version .30",
            "git version 2.x",
            "git version 2.-3",
            "git version -2.30",
            "git version 2.30x",
            "git version 99999999999.1",
            "git version 2.30.4294967296",
            "git version +2.30",
            "Git version 2.45.1",
            "version 2.45.1",
            "2.45.1",
            "hub version 2.45.1",
            "\ngit version 2.45.1",
        ] {
            let error = GitVersion::parse(text).expect_err(text);
            assert!(
                matches!(error, GitError::VersionUnreadable { .. }),
                "{text:?}: {error:?}"
            );
        }
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn versions_order_by_number_and_not_by_text() {
        let version = |text: &str| GitVersion::parse(text).expect("a version");
        assert!(version("git version 2.9.0") < GitVersion::MINIMUM);
        assert!(version("git version 2.29.9") < GitVersion::MINIMUM);
        assert!(version("git version 2.30.0") >= GitVersion::MINIMUM);
        assert!(version("git version 2.100.0") > version("git version 2.30.0"));
        assert!(version("git version 3.0.0") > version("git version 2.99.9"));
        assert!(version("git version 2.45.2") > version("git version 2.45.1"));
    }
    
    fn git_version(text: &str) -> FakeRunner {
        FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &["--version"], Ok(said(text)))
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn git_2_29_is_refused_and_2_30_and_2_45_are_accepted() {
        for older in [
            "git version 2.29.9\n",
            "git version 2.29.0\n",
            "git version 2.20.1\n",
            "git version 1.9.5\n",
            "git version 2.29.2.windows.1\n",
            "git version 2.29.3 (Apple Git-130)\n",
        ] {
            let error = require_version(&git_version(older)).expect_err(older);
            assert!(
                matches!(&error, GitError::VersionTooOld { found } if *found < GitVersion::MINIMUM),
                "{older:?}: {error:?}"
            );
            let message = error.to_string();
            assert!(message.contains("2.30.0"), "{message}");
            assert!(message.contains("--end-of-options"), "{message}");
        }
        for newer in [
            "git version 2.30.0\n",
            "git version 2.30.1\n",
            "git version 2.45.1\n",
            "git version 2.45.1.windows.1\n",
            "git version 2.39.5 (Apple Git-154)\n",
            "git version 3.1.0\n",
        ] {
            let version =
                require_version(&git_version(newer)).unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("{newer:?}: {e}"));
            assert!(version >= GitVersion::MINIMUM, "{newer:?}");
        }
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn the_version_question_is_exactly_git_version_in_the_c_locale() {
        let runner = git_version("git version 2.45.1\n");
        require_version(&runner).expect("a supported git");
        let calls = runner.calls();
        assert_eq!(calls.len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(calls[0].program(), Program::Git);
        assert_eq!(calls[0].args(), ["--version"]);
        assert_eq!(calls[0].limits(), &Limits::query());
        assert!(calls[0].limits().c_locale());
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn every_query_requests_the_documented_stream_and_execution_limits() {
        let mut runner = FakeRunner::new();
        let refname = "refs/remotes/origin/main";
        let revision = format!("{refname}^{{commit}}");
        let blob = format!("{COMMIT}:docs/capabilities/workplan-cli.md");
        let rows: &[(&[&str], &str)] = &[
            (&["--version"], "git version 2.45.1\n"),
            (&["rev-parse", "--show-prefix"], ""),
            (&["rev-parse", "--is-inside-work-tree"], "true\n"),
            (&["ls-files", "-z"], "docs/capabilities/workplan-cli.md\0"),
            (&["symbolic-ref", "refs/remotes/origin/HEAD"], refname),
            (
                &["rev-parse", "--verify", "--end-of-options", &revision],
                COMMIT,
            ),
            (&["merge-base", "HEAD", COMMIT], COMMIT),
            (&["ls-tree", "-r", "-z", "--full-tree", COMMIT], ""),
            (&["cat-file", "blob", &blob], "# Content\n"),
        ];
        for (args, output) in rows {
            runner = runner.on(Program::Git, args, Ok(said(output)));
        }
        require_version(&runner).expect("supported version");
        let prefix = show_prefix(&runner).expect("workspace prefix");
        assert_eq!(tracked_paths(&runner).unwrap().unwrap().len(), 1);
        let name = default_branch_ref(&runner).expect("default reference");
        let revision = resolve_commit(&runner, &name).expect("default commit");
        let base = merge_base(&runner, &revision).expect("merge base");
        assert_eq!(super::tree_at(&runner, &base, &prefix).unwrap().len(), 0);
        assert_eq!(
            blob_at(&runner, &base, &path("docs/capabilities/workplan-cli.md")).unwrap(),
            Some(b"# Content\n".to_vec())
        );
        let calls = runner.calls();
        assert_eq!(calls.len(), rows.len());
        for (call, (args, _)) in calls.iter().zip(rows) {
            assert_eq!(call.args(), *args);
            let bound = if args[0] == "cat-file" {
                MAX_TEXT_BYTES
            } else {
                16 * 1024 * 1024
            };
            assert_eq!(
                call.limits(),
                &Limits::new(bound, std::time::Duration::from_secs(300), true),
                "{}",
                call.command()
            );
        }
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn a_version_that_cannot_be_asked_or_read_is_an_error() {
        // Unreadable text, a failing exit, invalid UTF-8, a process that cannot run.
        let unreadable = require_version(&git_version("not git\n")).expect_err("unreadable");
        assert!(matches!(unreadable, GitError::VersionUnreadable { .. }));
        assert!(unreadable.to_string().contains("2.30.0"));
    
        let failing = FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &["--version"], Ok(failed(127, "boom\n")));
        let error = require_version(&failing).expect_err("a failing exit");
        assert!(matches!(&error, GitError::Failed { code: Some(127), stderr, .. } if stderr == "boom"));
    
        let bytes = FakeRunner::new().on(
            Program::Git,
            &["--version"],
            Ok(Output::new(Some(0), vec![0xff, 0xfe], Vec::new())),
        );
        assert!(matches!(
            require_version(&bytes),
            Err(GitError::VersionUnreadable { .. })
        ));
    
        let unscripted = require_version(&FakeRunner::new()).expect_err("nothing scripted");
        assert!(matches!(
            unscripted,
            GitError::Exec(ExecError::Unscripted { .. })
        ));
    }
    
    // ------------------------------------------------------------ tracked paths
    
    const INSIDE: [&str; 2] = ["rev-parse", "--is-inside-work-tree"];
    const LIST: [&str; 2] = ["ls-files", "-z"];
    
    #[test]
    fn the_tracked_paths_are_the_nul_separated_listing_of_the_index() {
        let runner = FakeRunner::new()
            .on(Program::Git, &INSIDE, Ok(said("true\n")))
            .on(
                Program::Git,
                &LIST,
                Ok(said("Cargo.toml\0docs/a b.md\0src/lib.rs\0")),
            );
        let paths = tracked_paths(&runner)
            .expect("a listing")
            .expect("inside a work tree");
        let names: Vec<&str> = paths.iter().map(raf_source::RelPath::as_str).collect();
        assert_eq!(names, ["Cargo.toml", "docs/a b.md", "src/lib.rs"]);
        let calls = runner.calls();
        assert_eq!(calls[0].args(), INSIDE);
        assert_eq!(calls[1].args(), LIST);
        for call in &calls {
            assert_eq!(call.program(), Program::Git);
            assert!(call.limits().c_locale(), "{}", call.command());
        }
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn a_directory_outside_a_work_tree_has_no_listing() {
        // `git` answers `false` inside a `.git` directory.
        let inside_git_dir = FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &INSIDE, Ok(said("false\n")));
        assert_eq!(tracked_paths(&inside_git_dir), Ok(None));
        assert_eq!(inside_git_dir.calls().len(), 1, "no listing is asked for");
    
        // And fails with `not a git repository` outside any repository.
        let outside = FakeRunner::new().on(
            Program::Git,
            &INSIDE,
            Ok(failed(
                128,
                "fatal: not a git repository (or any of the parent directories): .git\n",
            )),
        );
        assert_eq!(tracked_paths(&outside), Ok(None));
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn every_other_failure_of_the_listing_is_an_error_and_not_a_walk() {
        // A neighbour that is not "not a git repository" fails.
        let other = FakeRunner::new().on(
            Program::Git,
            &INSIDE,
            Ok(failed(
                128,
                "fatal: detected dubious ownership in repository\n",
            )),
        );
        let error = tracked_paths(&other).expect_err("another failure");
        assert!(
            matches!(
                &error,
                GitError::Failed {
                    code: Some(128),
                    ..
                }
            ),
            "{error:?}"
        );
        assert!(error.to_string().contains("dubious ownership"), "{error}");
    
        let odd = FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &INSIDE, Ok(said("maybe\n")));
        let error = tracked_paths(&odd).expect_err("an answer of neither word");
        assert!(matches!(error, GitError::Answer { .. }), "{error:?}");
    
        let broken_index = FakeRunner::new()
            .on(Program::Git, &INSIDE, Ok(said("true\n")))
            .on(
                Program::Git,
                &LIST,
                Ok(failed(128, "fatal: index file corrupt\n")),
            );
        let error = tracked_paths(&broken_index).expect_err("an unreadable index");
        assert!(error.to_string().contains("git ls-files"), "{error}");
        assert!(error.to_string().contains("index file corrupt"), "{error}");
    
        let cannot_start = FakeRunner::new();
        assert!(matches!(
            tracked_paths(&cannot_start),
            Err(GitError::Exec(ExecError::Unscripted { .. }))
        ));
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn a_tracked_path_must_be_utf8_and_a_relative_path_of_the_tree() {
        let listing = |bytes: &[u8]| {
            FakeRunner::new()
                .on(Program::Git, &INSIDE, Ok(said("true\n")))
                .on(
                    Program::Git,
                    &LIST,
                    Ok(Output::new(Some(0), bytes.to_vec(), Vec::new())),
                )
        };
        let error = tracked_paths(&listing(b"ok.md\0bad\xff.md\0")).expect_err("not UTF-8");
        assert!(matches!(error, GitError::NotUtf8 { .. }), "{error:?}");
        for bad in ["/etc/passwd", "../outside", "a/../../b"] {
            let error = tracked_paths(&listing(format!("{bad}\0").as_bytes())).expect_err(bad);
            assert!(
                matches!(&error, GitError::NotAPath { path, .. } if path == bad),
                "{bad}: {error:?}"
            );
        }
        // A neighbour: an empty listing is the empty set of paths.
        assert_eq!(tracked_paths(&listing(b"")), Ok(Some(Vec::new())));
    }
    
    // ------------------------------------------------------------ default branch
    
    const DEFAULT: [&str; 2] = ["symbolic-ref", "refs/remotes/origin/HEAD"];
    
    #[test]
    fn the_default_branch_is_the_full_reference_name() {
        let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(
            Program::Git,
            &DEFAULT,
            Ok(said("refs/remotes/origin/main\n")),
        );
        let name = default_branch_ref(&runner).expect("a default branch");
        assert_eq!(name.as_str(), "refs/remotes/origin/main");
        let calls = runner.calls();
        assert_eq!(calls.len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(calls[0].args(), DEFAULT);
        assert!(calls[0].limits().c_locale());
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn a_missing_default_branch_is_an_error_that_names_the_fix() {
        for stderr in [
            "fatal: ref refs/remotes/origin/HEAD is not a symbolic ref\n",
            "fatal: No such ref: refs/remotes/origin/HEAD\n",
            "",
        ] {
            let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &DEFAULT, Ok(failed(128, stderr)));
            let error = default_branch_ref(&runner).expect_err("no default branch");
            assert!(
                matches!(error, GitError::NoDefaultBranch { .. }),
                "{error:?}"
            );
            let message = error.to_string();
            assert!(
                message.contains("git remote set-head origin --auto"),
                "{message}"
            );
            assert!(message.contains("origin"), "{message}");
            assert!(message.contains(stderr.trim()), "{message}");
        }
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn an_answer_that_is_not_a_full_reference_name_never_becomes_one() {
        for answer in [
            "--output=x\n",
            "main\n",
            "origin/main\n",
            "refs/\n",
            "refs/remotes/origin/../x\n",
            "refs/remotes/origin/main x\n",
            "refs/remotes/origin/main\nrefs/remotes/origin/other\n",
            "refs/remotes/origin/a^b\n",
            "-refs/remotes/origin/main\n",
            "",
        ] {
            let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &DEFAULT, Ok(said(answer)));
            let error = default_branch_ref(&runner).expect_err(answer);
            assert!(
                matches!(error, GitError::Answer { .. }),
                "{answer:?}: {error:?}"
            );
        }
        // A branch named like an option is a valid reference name once it is
        // full: nothing but the `refs/` prefix keeps it from being an option.
        let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(
            Program::Git,
            &DEFAULT,
            Ok(said("refs/remotes/origin/--output=x\n")),
        );
        assert_eq!(
            default_branch_ref(&runner).expect("a valid name").as_str(),
            "refs/remotes/origin/--output=x"
        );
    }
    
    // ----------------------------------------------------------- resolve a ref
    
    #[test]
    fn a_reference_is_resolved_to_a_commit_after_the_end_of_options() {
        let name = reference("refs/remotes/origin/main");
        let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(
            Program::Git,
            &[
                "rev-parse",
                "--verify",
                "--end-of-options",
                "refs/remotes/origin/main^{commit}",
            ],
            Ok(said(&format!("{COMMIT}\n"))),
        );
        assert_eq!(resolve_commit(&runner, &name), Ok(commit(COMMIT)));
        assert_eq!(runner.calls().len(), 1);
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn a_reference_named_like_an_option_is_one_argument_after_the_end_of_options() {
        let name = reference("refs/remotes/origin/--output=x");
        let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(
            Program::Git,
            &[
                "rev-parse",
                "--verify",
                "--end-of-options",
                "refs/remotes/origin/--output=x^{commit}",
            ],
            Ok(said(&format!("{COMMIT}\n"))),
        );
        resolve_commit(&runner, &name).expect("resolved");
        let call = &runner.calls()[0];
        let marker = call
            .args()
            .iter()
            .position(|arg| arg == "--end-of-options")
            .expect("the marker");
        for (index, arg) in call.args().iter().enumerate() {
            if index > marker {
                assert!(arg.starts_with("refs/"), "{arg}");
            }
        }
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn a_reference_that_resolves_to_nothing_or_to_text_is_an_error() {
        let name = reference("refs/remotes/origin/main");
        let argv = [
            "rev-parse",
            "--verify",
            "--end-of-options",
            "refs/remotes/origin/main^{commit}",
        ];
        let gone = FakeRunner::new().on(
            Program::Git,
            &argv,
            Ok(failed(128, "fatal: Needed a single revision\n")),
        );
        let error = resolve_commit(&gone, &name).expect_err("does not resolve");
        assert!(
            matches!(
                &error,
                GitError::Failed {
                    code: Some(128),
                    ..
                }
            ),
            "{error:?}"
        );
        assert!(error.to_string().contains("Needed a single revision"));
        for answer in [
            "",
            "HEAD\n",
            "0123\n",
            "0123456789ABCDEF0123456789ABCDEF01234567\n",
        ] {
            let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &argv, Ok(said(answer)));
            assert!(
                matches!(resolve_commit(&runner, &name), Err(GitError::Answer { .. })),
                "{answer:?}"
            );
        }
        // Both hash lengths are commit ids.
        let long = "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef";
        let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &argv, Ok(said(&format!("{long}\n"))));
        assert_eq!(resolve_commit(&runner, &name), Ok(commit(long)));
    }
    
    // -------------------------------------------------------------- merge base
    
    #[test]
    fn the_merge_base_is_asked_of_head_and_the_commit() {
        let default = commit(COMMIT);
        let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(
            Program::Git,
            &["merge-base", "HEAD", COMMIT],
            Ok(said(&format!("{OTHER}\n"))),
        );
        assert_eq!(merge_base(&runner, &default), Ok(MergeBase(commit(OTHER))));
        assert_eq!(runner.calls().len(), 1);
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn histories_without_a_common_ancestor_and_odd_answers_are_errors() {
        let default = commit(COMMIT);
        let argv = ["merge-base", "HEAD", COMMIT];
        let none = FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &argv, Ok(failed(1, "")));
        let error = merge_base(&none, &default).expect_err("no merge base");
        assert!(matches!(&error, GitError::NoMergeBase { default: d } if *d == default));
        assert!(error.to_string().contains("no common ancestor"), "{error}");
    
        let broken = FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &argv, Ok(failed(128, "fatal: bad object\n")));
        assert!(matches!(
            merge_base(&broken, &default),
            Err(GitError::Failed {
                code: Some(128),
                ..
            })
        ));
        let two = FakeRunner::new().on(
            Program::Git,
            &argv,
            Ok(said(&format!("{COMMIT}\n{OTHER}\n"))),
        );
        assert!(matches!(
            merge_base(&two, &default),
            Err(GitError::Answer { .. })
        ));
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn the_merge_base_of_the_default_branch_is_three_questions_in_order_and_stops_at_the_first_failure()
    {
        let resolve = [
            "rev-parse",
            "--verify",
            "--end-of-options",
            "refs/remotes/origin/main^{commit}",
        ];
        let runner = FakeRunner::new()
            .on(
                Program::Git,
                &DEFAULT,
                Ok(said("refs/remotes/origin/main\n")),
            )
            .on(Program::Git, &resolve, Ok(said(&format!("{COMMIT}\n"))))
            .on(
                Program::Git,
                &["merge-base", "HEAD", COMMIT],
                Ok(said(&format!("{OTHER}\n"))),
            );
        let base = default_merge_base(&runner).expect("a merge base");
        assert_eq!(base, MergeBase(commit(OTHER)));
        assert_eq!(base.as_str(), OTHER);
        assert_eq!(base.to_string(), OTHER);
        assert_eq!(base.commit(), &commit(OTHER));
        let commands: Vec<String> = runner.calls().iter().map(Call::command).collect();
        assert_eq!(
            commands,
            [
                "git symbolic-ref refs/remotes/origin/HEAD".to_string(),
                "git rev-parse --verify --end-of-options refs/remotes/origin/main^{commit}".to_string(),
                format!("git merge-base HEAD {COMMIT}"),
            ]
        );
    
        // No default branch: the first question fails and nothing else is asked.
        let none = FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &DEFAULT, Ok(failed(128, "fatal: no\n")));
        assert!(matches!(
            default_merge_base(&none),
            Err(GitError::NoDefaultBranch { .. })
        ));
        assert_eq!(none.calls().len(), 1);
    }
    
    // ----------------------------------------------------------------- blob_at
    
    fn blob_argv() -> [String; 3] {
        [
            "cat-file".to_string(),
            "blob".to_string(),
            format!("{COMMIT}:docs/capabilities/workplan-cli.md"),
        ]
    }
    
    fn blob_runner(answer: Result<Output, ExecError>) -> FakeRunner {
        let argv = blob_argv();
        let words: Vec<&str> = argv.iter().map(String::as_str).collect();
        FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &words, answer)
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn a_blob_is_read_from_the_merge_base_in_one_argument() {
        let runner = blob_runner(Ok(said("# Title\n")));
        let bytes = blob_at(
            &runner,
            &MergeBase(commit(COMMIT)),
            &path("docs/capabilities/workplan-cli.md"),
        )
        .expect("a blob");
        assert_eq!(bytes.as_deref(), Some(b"# Title\n".as_slice()));
        let call = &runner.calls()[0];
        assert_eq!(call.args(), blob_argv());
        assert_eq!(call.limits().max_output(), MAX_TEXT_BYTES);
        assert!(call.limits().c_locale());
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn a_blob_over_one_mebibyte_is_an_error_and_never_cut() {
        let at = path("docs/capabilities/workplan-cli.md");
        let exact = blob_runner(Ok(Output::new(
            Some(0),
            vec![b'a'; MAX_TEXT_BYTES],
            Vec::new(),
        )));
        let bytes = blob_at(&exact, &MergeBase(commit(COMMIT)), &at)
            .expect("exactly the bound passes")
            .expect("a blob");
        assert_eq!(bytes.len(), MAX_TEXT_BYTES);
    
        let over = blob_runner(Ok(Output::new(
            Some(0),
            vec![b'a'; MAX_TEXT_BYTES + 1],
            Vec::new(),
        )));
        let error = blob_at(&over, &MergeBase(commit(COMMIT)), &at).expect_err("one byte over");
        assert!(
            matches!(&error, GitError::BlobTooLarge { limit, path } if *limit == MAX_TEXT_BYTES && *path == at),
            "{error:?}"
        );
        assert!(error.to_string().contains("1048576"), "{error}");
    
        // The same error from the real runner's own report of an overflow.
        let overflow = blob_runner(Err(ExecError::OutputTooLarge {
            command: "git cat-file".to_string(),
            stream: Stream::Stdout,
            limit: MAX_TEXT_BYTES,
            elapsed: std::time::Duration::from_millis(40),
        }));
        assert!(matches!(
            blob_at(&overflow, &MergeBase(commit(COMMIT)), &at),
            Err(GitError::BlobTooLarge { .. })
        ));
        // An overflow of standard error is not a large blob.
        let noisy = blob_runner(Err(ExecError::OutputTooLarge {
            command: "git cat-file".to_string(),
            stream: Stream::Stderr,
            limit: MAX_TEXT_BYTES,
            elapsed: std::time::Duration::from_millis(40),
        }));
        assert!(matches!(
            blob_at(&noisy, &MergeBase(commit(COMMIT)), &at),
            Err(GitError::Exec(ExecError::OutputTooLarge { .. }))
        ));
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn a_path_missing_at_the_merge_base_is_none_and_other_failures_are_errors() {
        let at = path("docs/capabilities/workplan-cli.md");
        for stderr in [
            format!("fatal: path 'docs/capabilities/workplan-cli.md' does not exist in '{COMMIT}'\n"),
            format!(
                "fatal: path 'docs/capabilities/workplan-cli.md' exists on disk, but not in '{COMMIT}'\n"
            ),
        ] {
            let runner = blob_runner(Ok(failed(128, &stderr)));
            assert_eq!(
                blob_at(&runner, &MergeBase(commit(COMMIT)), &at),
                Ok(None),
                "{stderr}"
            );
        }
        for stderr in [
            format!("fatal: git cat-file {COMMIT}:docs/capabilities/workplan-cli.md: bad file\n"),
            "fatal: bad object\n".to_string(),
            String::new(),
        ] {
            let runner = blob_runner(Ok(failed(128, &stderr)));
            let error = blob_at(&runner, &MergeBase(commit(COMMIT)), &at).expect_err(&stderr);
            assert!(
                matches!(error, GitError::Failed { .. }),
                "{stderr}: {error:?}"
            );
        }
    }
    
    // ------------------------------------------------------------------ prefix
    
    const PREFIX: [&str; 2] = ["rev-parse", "--show-prefix"];
    
    fn prefix_of(answer: Output) -> Result<super::Prefix, GitError> {
        show_prefix(&FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &PREFIX, Ok(answer)))
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn the_prefix_is_empty_at_the_top_and_a_path_with_a_slash_below_it() {
        let top = prefix_of(said("\n")).expect("the top");
        assert!(top.is_top());
        assert_eq!(top.as_str(), "");
        assert_eq!(top, super::Prefix::default());
    
        let one = prefix_of(said("sub/\n")).expect("one level");
        assert!(!one.is_top());
        assert_eq!(one.as_str(), "sub/");
        let deep = prefix_of(said("services/api-v2/crate_1.0/\n")).expect("three levels");
        assert_eq!(deep.as_str(), "services/api-v2/crate_1.0/");
    
        // The prefix goes before the document path, and before nothing at the top.
        let document = path("docs/capabilities/x.md");
        assert_eq!(
            deep.join(&document).expect("joined").as_str(),
            "services/api-v2/crate_1.0/docs/capabilities/x.md"
        );
        assert_eq!(top.join(&document).expect("joined"), document);
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn the_prefix_is_asked_with_exactly_its_argv_in_the_c_locale() {
        let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Git, &PREFIX, Ok(said("sub/\n")));
        show_prefix(&runner).expect("a prefix");
        let calls = runner.calls();
        assert_eq!(calls.len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(calls[0].args(), PREFIX);
        assert!(calls[0].limits().c_locale());
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn a_prefix_the_source_path_pattern_cannot_carry_is_refused_with_its_neighbour_accepted() {
        for answer in [
            "my docs/",
            "sub",
            "../up/",
            "./sub/",
            "a//b/",
            "/abs/",
            "-opts/",
            "caf\u{e9}/",
            "a b/c/",
            "a;b/",
            "/",
        ] {
            let error = prefix_of(said(&format!("{answer}\n"))).expect_err(answer);
            assert!(
                matches!(&error, GitError::Answer { .. }),
                "{answer}: {error:?}"
            );
        }
        // Two lines are not one prefix.
        assert!(matches!(
            prefix_of(said("a/\nb/\n")),
            Err(GitError::Answer { .. })
        ));
        // The neighbours: a dot inside a name, a hyphen, an underscore, a digit.
        for answer in ["a.b/", "a-b/", "_a/", "9/", "a/b/c/"] {
            prefix_of(said(&format!("{answer}\n"))).unwrap_or_else(|error| panic!("{answer}: {error}"));
        }
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn a_failing_prefix_question_is_an_error() {
        let error =
            prefix_of(failed(128, "fatal: not a git repository\n")).expect_err("not a work tree");
        assert!(
            matches!(
                error,
                GitError::Failed {
                    code: Some(128),
                    ..
                }
            ),
            "{error:?}"
        );
    }

    // SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2026 Blackcat Informatics® Inc. <paudley@blackcatinformatics.ca>
    // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT OR Apache-2.0 OR MulanPSL-2.0
    
    //! The steps of a change: opening its record, reading what it touches,
    //! reconciling it with the documents, verifying what it binds and completing
    //! it.
    //!
    //! [`Workplan::new_change`](crate::Workplan::new_change) creates `work/changes/<slug>.md` for a slug, the
    //! capabilities it changes and the sections it affects, and says what the author
    //! does next: the PROPOSED block to place and the state each listed capability
    //! needs while the block is there ([`NewChange`]). It writes exactly one file,
    //! created exclusively and never over an existing one, and reads the record
    //! back through the loader before it reports success. Given a copyright text and
    //! a licence identifier ([`NewChangeRequest::copyright`] and
    //! [`NewChangeRequest::licence`], both or neither) it opens the record with the two
    //! SPDX comment lines, so a repository that checks licences accepts the file as
    //! it is.
    //!
    //! [`Workplan::impact`](crate::Workplan::impact) returns what a change reaches as one [`Impact`] value:
    //! the capabilities it changes, the sections it affects, the bindings of those
    //! capabilities, the capabilities that depend on them and the sections that
    //! link to the affected sections, because those readers are affected too. It
    //! writes nothing and is a pure function of the loaded documents.
    //!
    //! [`Lifecycle::open`] is the entry point of the commands that start a process.
    //! It asks `git --version` before any other process and refuses a Git older than
    //! 2.30, then opens the repository ([`Gate::open`](crate::Gate::open)).
    //! [`Lifecycle::reconcile`] returns the whole obligation list of a change: the
    //! static kinds of [`Workplan::reconcile`](crate::Workplan::reconcile) and the
    //! three that need a process.
    //!
    //! * `section-untouched` compares each section in `affects` with the file at the
    //!   merge base of `HEAD` and the default branch of `origin`
    //!   ([`git::default_merge_base`](crate::git::default_merge_base) and
    //!   [`git::blob_at`](crate::git::blob_at), the only revision it reads at being
    //!   a [`git::MergeBase`](crate::git::MergeBase)). A section is touched when its
    //!   authored text differs ([`section_digest`]: the content of generated blocks
    //!   is left out on both sides, fences and PROPOSED blocks are not); a file that
    //!   is absent at the merge base counts as touched; a merge-base file that
    //!   exists and does not parse is an error; a repository with no default branch
    //!   is an error that names `git remote set-head origin --auto`. On the default
    //!   branch itself the merge base is `HEAD`, so every affected section is owed.
    //!   `git` names a file from the top of the work tree, so [`Lifecycle::open`]
    //!   asks `git rev-parse --show-prefix` and a workspace below the top of its
    //!   repository reads its documents at that prefix; a prefix the source-path
    //!   pattern cannot carry is an error, and a linked work tree is its own top.
    //! * `evidence-changed` reads, at the same merge base and with the same
    //!   `git cat-file blob` row, the evidence record of each id a claim of a listed
    //!   capability names. A record that passed there and now fails, holds a value
    //!   over its limit or the limit of the merge base, has lost a limit, or no
    //!   longer loads is owed at each claim that names it; a record there that does
    //!   not read as an evidence record is an error. The merge base is asked for
    //!   once, and only when a section or a claim needs it
    //!   (`Lifecycle::process_obligations` assembles the kinds that need a process
    //!   for both `reconcile` and `complete`).
    //! * `example-failing` builds and runs the example targets and documentation
    //!   tests of the capabilities the change lists, through the
    //!   [`Executor`](crate::bound::Executor); one that fails, runs zero tests or
    //!   proves nothing is owed ([`RunProblem`](crate::obligation::RunProblem)).
    //!   Test bindings are not run by `reconcile`.
    //!
    //! [`verify`] runs the bound tests and examples of one change, or of every
    //! capability, and returns a [`VerifyReport`] of per-binding
    //! [`Evidence`](crate::bound::Evidence). It needs no Git, goes through no
    //! version gate and writes nothing.
    //!
    //! # Completing a change
    //!
    //! [`Lifecycle::complete`] is the transition from an open change to a delivered
    //! one, and returns a [`Completion`]: the record was removed, or the change is
    //! refused with every [`Failure`] and nothing was written. The order of its
    //! steps is part of its contract.
    //!
    //! 1. The static conditions are judged, all of them, with no early exit: the
    //!    obligation list (every kind except `example-failing`, which needs a run),
    //!    the outcome of each item (a `moved` outcome names a record that exists),
    //!    the PROPOSED blocks of the change in any capability document, loaded or
    //!    not, the generated blocks (`docs sync` would write nothing), a current
    //!    accepted assessment of each authored section in `affects` (condition 6,
    //!    see [`assessment`](crate::assessment)), and `check` of the repository as
    //!    it will be once the writes are done. That
    //!    repository has no record, so the state rules are judged there: a
    //!    `delivered` capability beside its still-open record is exactly what
    //!    `complete` resolves and is not a failure, while a capability that is not
    //!    `delivered` and is listed by no other open record is `no-open-record`.
    //!    The author sets `state: delivered` before running `complete`; it never
    //!    edits prose.
    //! 2. Only when no static condition failed does it run the bound tests and
    //!    examples of the listed capabilities, through the
    //!    [`Executor`](crate::bound::Executor). A static failure therefore spends no
    //!    process on `cargo`. A capability with empty `bindings.tests` and
    //!    `bindings.examples` satisfies this condition only while it is `designed`
    //!    or `considered`. A failing example is also an `example-failing`
    //!    obligation, so it is reported under conditions 1 and 5.
    //! 3. The repository is opened again and the static conditions are judged again,
    //!    because the documents and the source can change while `cargo` runs. A
    //!    failure, or a different set of bindings to run, refuses.
    //! 4. The writes. They are the generated blocks that removing the record stales
    //!    (the `Changed by` rows of the relationship tables), rewritten through
    //!    [`Workplan::write`](crate::Workplan::write), which stages every file and
    //!    then renames; and then, as the very last step, the removal of
    //!    `work/changes/<slug>.md`. Nothing else is written. If the rewrite fails the
    //!    record is not removed; if the removal fails after the rewrite the
    //!    [`LifecycleError::Remove`] names the files already rewritten. A removed
    //!    record is therefore never found beside stale blocks.
    
    mod changed;
    mod complete;
    mod completion;
    mod create;
    mod examples;
    mod impact;
    mod session;
    mod touched;
    mod untouched;
    mod verification;

    pub use create::{
        AffectsProblem, CapabilityGuidance, NewChange, NewChangeError, NewChangeRequest, StateAdvice,
    };
    pub use impact::{Dependant, Impact, LinkingSection};
    pub use session::{Lifecycle, LifecycleError, Side};
    pub use untouched::{SectionDigest, section_digest};
    pub use verification::{VerifyReport, verify};

    // SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2026 Blackcat Informatics® Inc. <paudley@blackcatinformatics.ca>
