    //! `evidence-changed`: an evidence record that a claim of a listed capability
    //! names, which passed at the merge base and has changed since.
    //!
    //! The record of each id the claims name is read once at the merge base, at
    //! `work/evidence/<slug>.md` below the workspace's prefix, with the one
    //! `git cat-file blob` row of `section-untouched` and its 1 MiB bound. A record
    //! that is not there, or whose verdict there is `fail`, gives nothing to
    //! compare: the claim is judged by `claim-unsupported` alone. A record there
    //! that does not read as an evidence record is an error. Otherwise each change
    //! is owed at each claim that names the record: the verdict went from `pass`
    //! to `fail`, a value is now over a limit the record or the record at the
    //! merge base states, a limit of the record at the merge base is gone, or no
    //! record loads under the id now.
    
    use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};
    
    use raf_okf::{
        Capability, Document, EvidenceRecord, EvidenceVerdict, Measure, Profile, RafDocument,
    };
    
    use crate::evidence::record_path;
    use crate::exec::Runner;
    use crate::git::{MergeBase, Prefix};
    use crate::obligation::trace::claim_part;
    use crate::obligation::{
        EvidenceChange, Needed, Obligation, ObligationKind, OverLimit, Place, Subject,
    };
    use crate::{Loaded, Opened};
    
    use super::Impact;
    use super::session::{LifecycleError, Side};
    use super::untouched;
    
    /// The `evidence-changed` obligations of a change.
    ///
    /// Nothing is asked of `git` when no claim of a listed capability names an
    /// evidence record.
    pub(super) fn raise(
        opened: &Opened,
        runner: &dyn Runner,
        prefix: &Prefix,
        impact: &Impact,
        base: &mut Option<MergeBase>,
    ) -> Result<Vec<Obligation>, LifecycleError> {
        let plan = opened.workplan();
        let listed: Vec<&Loaded<Capability>> = plan
            .capabilities()
            .iter()
            .filter(|cap| impact.capabilities.contains(&cap.value().header.id))
            .collect();
        let named: BTreeSet<String> = listed
            .iter()
            .flat_map(|cap| cap.body().claims())
            .flat_map(|claim| claim.evidence().iter())
            .map(|slug| format!("evidence:{slug}"))
            .collect();
        if named.is_empty() {
            return Ok(Vec::new());
        }
        let base = untouched::merge_base(base, runner)?;
        let mut changes: BTreeMap<&str, Vec<EvidenceChange>> = BTreeMap::new();
        for id in &named {
            let path = record_path(id);
            let Some(then) = record_at(runner, base, prefix, &path)? else {
                continue;
            };
            if then.verdict != EvidenceVerdict::Pass {
                continue;
            }
            let now = plan
                .evidence()
                .iter()
                .find(|record| record.value().header.id == *id)
                .map(Loaded::value);
            changes.insert(id, compare(&then, now));
        }
        let mut owed = Vec::new();
        for cap in listed {
            for claim in cap.body().claims() {
                for slug in claim.evidence() {
                    let id = format!("evidence:{slug}");
                    for change in changes.get(id.as_str()).into_iter().flatten() {
                        owed.push(Obligation {
                            kind: ObligationKind::EvidenceChanged,
                            subject: Subject::Capability(cap.value().header.id.clone()),
                            section: claim_part(claim.section()),
                            location: Place {
                                path: cap.path().to_string(),
                                line: Some(claim.line()),
                            },
                            evidence: Needed::ReviewedClaim {
                                evidence: id.clone(),
                                change: change.clone(),
                            },
                        });
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(owed)
    }
    
    /// What changed from the passing record `then` to `now`, or that it is gone.
    fn compare(then: &EvidenceRecord, now: Option<&EvidenceRecord>) -> Vec<EvidenceChange> {
        let Some(now) = now else {
            return vec![EvidenceChange::Removed];
        };
        let mut changes = Vec::new();
        if now.verdict == EvidenceVerdict::Fail {
            changes.push(EvidenceChange::Failed);
        }
        let limit_of = |limits: &[Measure], name: &str| -> Option<Measure> {
            limits.iter().find(|limit| limit.name == name).cloned()
        };
        for value in &now.values {
            // The tighter of the two limits a value has, the record's own and the
            // one of the merge base.
            let tightest = [
                limit_of(&now.limits, &value.name),
                limit_of(&then.limits, &value.name),
            ]
            .into_iter()
            .flatten()
            .filter(|limit| value.value.exceeds(&limit.value))
            .min_by(|a, b| a.value.compare(&b.value));
            if let Some(limit) = tightest {
                changes.push(EvidenceChange::OverLimit(OverLimit {
                    name: value.name.clone(),
                    value: value.value.text().to_string(),
                    limit: limit.value.text().to_string(),
                }));
            }
        }
        for limit in &then.limits {
            if limit_of(&now.limits, &limit.name).is_none() {
                changes.push(EvidenceChange::LimitRemoved(limit.name.clone()));
            }
        }
        changes
    }
    
    /// The evidence record at `path` at the merge base, or `None` when no file is
    /// there.
    fn record_at(
        runner: &dyn Runner,
        base: &MergeBase,
        prefix: &Prefix,
        path: &str,
    ) -> Result<Option<EvidenceRecord>, LifecycleError> {
        let Some(bytes) = untouched::blob(runner, base, prefix, path)? else {
            return Ok(None);
        };
        let refused = |reason: String| LifecycleError::Document {
            path: path.to_string(),
            side: Side::MergeBase,
            reason,
        };
        let text = String::from_utf8(bytes).map_err(|_| refused("is not UTF-8 text".to_string()))?;
        let document = Document::parse(&text).map_err(|error| refused(error.to_string()))?;
        match Profile.validate(&document, path) {
            Ok(RafDocument::Evidence(record)) => Ok(Some(record)),
            Ok(_) => Err(refused("is not an evidence record".to_string())),
            Err(violations) => Err(refused(format!(
                "is not a valid evidence record: {}",
                violations
                    .iter()
                    .map(ToString::to_string)
                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                    .join("; ")
            ))),
        }
    }
    
    #[cfg(test)]
    mod tests {
        use raf_okf::{
            Actor, EvidenceRecord, EvidenceSubject, EvidenceVerdict, Input, InputKind, Measure, Number,
        };
    
        use super::compare;
        use crate::obligation::{EvidenceChange, OverLimit};
    
        fn measure(name: &str, value: &str) -> Measure {
            Measure::new(name, Number::parse(value).expect("a number"))
        }
    
        fn record(
            verdict: EvidenceVerdict,
            values: &[(&str, &str)],
            limits: &[(&str, &str)],
        ) -> EvidenceRecord {
            let mut record = EvidenceRecord::new(
                "bound",
                EvidenceSubject::Command,
                vec![Input::new(InputKind::Symbol, "demo::run", "0".repeat(64))],
                verdict,
                Actor::Agent,
                vec!["cap:demo".to_string()],
            );
            record.values = values.iter().map(|(n, v)| measure(n, v)).collect();
            record.limits = limits.iter().map(|(n, v)| measure(n, v)).collect();
            record
        }
    
        fn over(name: &str, value: &str, limit: &str) -> EvidenceChange {
            EvidenceChange::OverLimit(OverLimit {
                name: name.to_string(),
                value: value.to_string(),
                limit: limit.to_string(),
            })
        }
    
        #[test]
        fn a_value_over_a_limit_by_less_than_a_float_tells_apart_is_a_change() {
            let one_e400 = format!("1{}", "0".repeat(400));
            let two_e400 = format!("2{}", "0".repeat(400));
            for (over_text, limit, under) in [
                ("9007199254740993", "9007199254740992", "9007199254740991"),
                ("0.30000000000000001", "0.3", "0.29999999999999999"),
                ("1.0000000000000000001", "1", "0.9999999999999999999"),
                (two_e400.as_str(), one_e400.as_str(), &one_e400[..400]),
            ] {
                let then = record(
                    EvidenceVerdict::Pass,
                    &[("peak", under)],
                    &[("peak", limit)],
                );
                // The limit of the merge base is loosened, and the value passes it.
                let now = record(
                    EvidenceVerdict::Pass,
                    &[("peak", over_text)],
                    &[("peak", &format!("{limit}1"))],
                );
                assert_eq!(compare(&then, Some(&now)), [over("peak", over_text, limit)]);
                for neighbour in [limit, under] {
                    let now = record(
                        EvidenceVerdict::Pass,
                        &[("peak", neighbour)],
                        &[("peak", limit)],
                    );
                    assert_eq!(compare(&then, Some(&now)), [], "{neighbour}");
                }
            }
        }
    
        #[test]
        fn an_unchanged_or_better_record_owes_nothing() {
            let then = record(EvidenceVerdict::Pass, &[("peak", "41")], &[("peak", "64")]);
            assert_eq!(compare(&then, Some(&then)), []);
            let tighter = record(EvidenceVerdict::Pass, &[("peak", "30")], &[("peak", "32")]);
            assert_eq!(compare(&then, Some(&tighter)), []);
        }
    
        #[test]
        fn each_change_is_named() {
            let then = record(EvidenceVerdict::Pass, &[("peak", "41")], &[("peak", "64")]);
            assert_eq!(compare(&then, None), [EvidenceChange::Removed]);
            let failing = record(EvidenceVerdict::Fail, &[("peak", "80")], &[("peak", "64")]);
            assert_eq!(
                compare(&then, Some(&failing)),
                [EvidenceChange::Failed, over("peak", "80", "64")]
            );
            let unbounded = record(EvidenceVerdict::Pass, &[("peak", "41")], &[]);
            assert_eq!(
                compare(&then, Some(&unbounded)),
                [EvidenceChange::LimitRemoved("peak".to_string())]
            );
            // A limit loosened past the old one: the value is over the limit of
            // the merge base, which is named.
            let loosened = record(EvidenceVerdict::Pass, &[("peak", "80")], &[("peak", "100")]);
            assert_eq!(compare(&then, Some(&loosened)), [over("peak", "80", "64")]);
        }
    }
  in crates/raf-workplan/src/lifecycle/complete.rs
    // SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2026 Blackcat Informatics® Inc. <paudley@blackcatinformatics.ca>
    // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT OR Apache-2.0 OR MulanPSL-2.0
    
    //! `complete`: the transition from an open change to a delivered one.
    //!
    //! The order is the order of the Contract and is not interchangeable.
    //!
    //! 1. The static conditions are judged, all of them, and every failure is
    //!    collected: the obligation list of `reconcile` (every kind except
    //!    `example-failing`, which needs a run), the outcome of each item, the
    //!    PROPOSED blocks of the change, the generated blocks, the assessment of
    //!    each authored section in `affects`, and `check` of the repository as it
    //!    will be once the record is gone.
    //! 2. Only when none failed are the bound tests and examples run, so a
    //!    repository with a documentation fault spends no process on `cargo`.
    //! 3. The repository is opened again and the static conditions are judged
    //!    again, because the documents and the source can change while `cargo`
    //!    runs. Any failure, or a different set of bindings to run, refuses.
    //! 4. The generated blocks the removal stales are rewritten, and then, as the
    //!    very last step, the record is removed.
    
    use std::fs;
    use std::io;
    
    use raf_okf::{DocType, Location};
    use raf_source::{EntryKind, RelPath};
    
    use crate::bound::{BoundOutcome, Evidence, Executor};
    use crate::exec::Runner;
    use crate::files::{self, Target};
    use crate::finding::kind;
    use crate::generated;
    use crate::lookup::bare_slug;
    use crate::obligation::{ObligationKind, Reconciled};
    use crate::{Binding, BindingKind, Finding, Opened, SyncError, SyncOptions, SyncReport, Workplan};
    
    use super::completion::{Completion, Failure};
    use super::examples;
    use super::session::{Lifecycle, LifecycleError, Side};
    use super::touched;
    
    /// The static conditions judged over one opening of the repository.
    struct Evaluation {
        /// Every failure, in the order of the conditions.
        failures: Vec<Failure>,
        /// The test and example bindings of the listed capabilities, in the order
        /// they run.
        runs: Vec<Binding>,
        /// The path of the record.
        record: RelPath,
        /// The generated blocks the removal stales, rewritten as the sync of the
        /// repository without the record writes them. Empty while a failure
        /// stands.
        rewrite: SyncReport,
    }
    
    /// What identifies a binding that runs.
    type RunKey = (String, BindingKind, String);
    
    fn run_keys(runs: &[Binding]) -> Vec<RunKey> {
        runs.iter()
            .map(|binding| {
                (
                    binding.capability().to_string(),
                    binding.kind(),
                    binding.path().to_string(),
                )
            })
            .collect()
    }
    
    /// Condition 6: an `assessment-missing`, `assessment-stale` or
    /// `assessment-rejected` obligation of `owed` is a section without a current
    /// accepted assessment.
    fn assessment_failures(owed: &Reconciled) -> impl Iterator<Item = Failure> + '_ {
        owed.obligations()
            .iter()
            .filter(|owed| {
                matches!(
                    owed.kind,
                    ObligationKind::AssessmentMissing
                        | ObligationKind::AssessmentStale
                        | ObligationKind::AssessmentRejected
                )
            })
            .cloned()
            .map(Failure::Assessment)
    }
    
    impl<R: Runner> Lifecycle<R> {
        /// Complete the change `change`: refuse with every reason it cannot be
        /// completed, or rewrite the generated blocks the removal stales and
        /// remove `work/changes/<slug>.md`.
        ///
        /// `change` is the slug, with or without the `change:` prefix. The six
        /// conditions of the completion gate are judged as the module documents:
        ///
        /// 1. the obligation list of [`Lifecycle::reconcile`](Lifecycle::reconcile)
        ///    is empty (every kind, `contract_effect: none` leaving out the
        ///    untouched contract alone; the list also holds
        ///    `contract-effect-understated`);
        /// 2. every item has an `outcome`, and a `moved` outcome names a change
        ///    record that exists; a `dropped` or `moved` item
        ///    also has a `reason` (`outcome-without-reason`);
        /// 3. no PROPOSED block of the change remains in any capability document,
        ///    whether the document loads or not;
        /// 4. `docs sync` would write nothing;
        /// 5. each test of `bindings.tests` and each example of
        ///    `bindings.examples` of a listed capability passes, by the proof its
        ///    kind requires ([`Executor`]). A capability with empty lists satisfies
        ///    this only while it is `designed` or `considered`; in any other state
        ///    the empty list is a `binding-missing` obligation of condition 1;
        /// 6. each authored section in `affects` has a current assessment with the
        ///    verdict `accepted` ([`assessment`](crate::assessment)): a section
        ///    with no assessment, one whose inputs moved, and one that is current
        ///    and `rejected` each refuse, whether the assessor was a human or an
        ///    agent. A section that holds only generated blocks would need none,
        ///    but the profile refuses one, so every section of a loaded capability
        ///    is authored.
        ///    This is judged with the other static conditions, before any bound
        ///    test or example runs.
        ///
        /// A failing example is both an `example-failing` obligation (condition 1)
        /// and a failure of condition 5, and a section without a current accepted
        /// assessment is one or more `assessment-*` obligations (condition 1), one
        /// `assessment-stale` for each input that moved, each also a failure of
        /// condition 6; the headline of the refusal counts the section once.
        ///
        /// The repository without the record must also pass `check`, apart from
        /// the generated blocks the removal stales (the `Changed by` rows of the
        /// relationship tables), which `complete` rewrites. This is where the
        /// state of a listed capability is judged: a capability that is not
        /// `delivered` and is listed by no other open record is `no-open-record`,
        /// so the author sets `state: delivered` before running `complete`.
        /// `complete` never edits prose, and it does not judge the repository
        /// before the removal by the state rules: a `delivered` capability beside
        /// its still-open record is exactly what it resolves.
        ///
        /// That check fails on every finding in the repository, not only on the
        /// ones about the listed capabilities: a broken link in an unrelated
        /// capability blocks completion, and so does a link in prose to the record
        /// itself, which is broken once the record is removed.
        ///
        /// An `outcome` that is not `implemented`, `dropped` or
        /// `moved:change:<slug>` is a value the profile refuses and makes the
        /// record not load, so `complete` returns the error of
        /// [`Workplan::change`] and not a condition-2 refusal, as
        /// [`Lifecycle::reconcile`](Lifecycle::reconcile) does. Condition 2
        /// refuses an item with no outcome and a `moved` outcome whose target is
        /// missing.
        ///
        /// ```rust,no_run
        /// use raf_workplan::exec::Programs;
        /// use raf_workplan::lifecycle::Lifecycle;
        ///
        /// let lifecycle = Lifecycle::open(Programs::new("."))?;
        /// let completion = lifecycle.complete("incremental-parsing")?;
        /// print!("{completion}");
        /// std::process::exit(i32::from(!completion.is_completed()));
        /// # Ok::<(), raf_workplan::lifecycle::LifecycleError>(())
        /// ```
        ///
        /// # Writes
        ///
        /// A refusal publishes no tool-owned files. A completion writes the generated
        /// blocks that the removal stales (through [`Workplan::write`], which stages
        /// every file and then renames) and then removes the record, as the very
        /// last step. If the rewrite fails the record is not removed. If the
        /// removal fails after the rewrite, the error says which files were
        /// rewritten. These guarantees concern this method's own publication.
        /// Trusted Cargo builds, tests and examples can write workspace files;
        /// they are not sandboxed. Git can fetch missing objects into its metadata
        /// in a partial clone without changing working files or the index.
        ///
        /// A document changed between the second evaluation and the write is not
        /// detected: [`Workplan::write`] replaces a file without comparing it with
        /// the text the rewrite was computed from.
        ///
        /// # Errors
        ///
        /// [`LifecycleError::Change`] and [`LifecycleError::Reconcile`] when the
        /// record or a listed capability does not load; [`LifecycleError::Git`]
        /// and [`LifecycleError::Document`] when a section cannot be compared;
        /// [`LifecycleError::Exec`] when a process of a bound run cannot be
        /// started or read to its end; [`LifecycleError::RecordRefused`] when
        /// `work` or `work/changes` is a symbolic link or not a directory, or the
        /// record is not a regular file there, judged with each evaluation, the
        /// first before anything runs and the second after the bound runs and
        /// before the writes; [`LifecycleError::Gate`] when the
        /// repository cannot be opened again; [`LifecycleError::Rewrite`] and
        /// [`LifecycleError::Remove`] when a write fails. Each is the tool being
        /// unable to give an answer, which is not a refusal.
        pub fn complete(&self, change: &str) -> Result<Completion, LifecycleError> {
            let slug = bare_slug(change);
    
            // The source index at the merge base is loaded once for both
            // judgements: the files of one commit do not change, and the second
            // judgement asks for the merge base again and loads it again only when
            // it, or the packages it is read from, differ.
            let mut kept = None;
            let before = self.evaluate(&self.opened, slug, false, &mut kept)?;
            if !before.failures.is_empty() {
                return Ok(Completion::Refused {
                    failures: before.failures,
                    evidence: Vec::new(),
                });
            }
    
            let (failing, evidence) = self.run_bound(&self.opened, &before.runs)?;
            if !failing.is_empty() {
                return Ok(Completion::Refused {
                    failures: failing,
                    evidence,
                });
            }
    
            // The documents and the source can change while `cargo` runs, and
            // `Opened` holds the source as it was.
            let opened = self.gate.open()?;
            let after = self.evaluate(&opened, slug, true, &mut kept)?;
            let different = run_keys(&after.runs) != run_keys(&before.runs);
            if different || !after.failures.is_empty() {
                let what = if different {
                    "the bound tests and examples to run differ from the ones that ran"
                } else {
                    "a condition that held before the bound tests and examples ran fails now"
                };
                let mut failures = vec![Failure::Changed {
                    what: what.to_string(),
                }];
                failures.extend(after.failures);
                return Ok(Completion::Refused { failures, evidence });
            }
    
            Self::write(&opened, &after.record, &after.rewrite, evidence)
        }
    
        /// Judge the static conditions over `opened`; `ran` says whether the
        /// bound tests and examples have run, which a refused way to the record
        /// reports, and `kept` holds the source index at the merge base of an
        /// earlier judgement.
        fn evaluate(
            &self,
            opened: &Opened,
            slug: &str,
            ran: bool,
            kept: &mut Option<touched::BaseIndex>,
        ) -> Result<Evaluation, LifecycleError> {
            let plan = opened.workplan();
            let record = plan.change(slug)?;
            let fixed = plan.reconcile(slug)?;
            let impact = plan.impact(slug)?;
            let record_path =
                RelPath::new(record.path()).map_err(|error| LifecycleError::Document {
                    path: record.path().to_string(),
                    side: Side::WorkingTree,
                    reason: error.to_string(),
                })?;
            // The record is removed as the last step, so the way to it is judged
            // as every write of the crate judges it, before anything runs and
            // again before the writes.
            Self::judge_record(opened, &record_path, ran)?;
    
            // Condition 1, all kinds that need no run of `cargo`.
            let mut obligations = fixed.obligations().to_vec();
            obligations.extend(self.process_obligations(
                opened,
                &plan,
                &impact,
                record.value().contract_effect,
                false,
                kept,
            )?);
            let owed = Reconciled::new(impact.change.clone(), obligations);
            let mut failures: Vec<Failure> = owed
                .obligations()
                .iter()
                .cloned()
                .map(Failure::Owed)
                .collect();
    
            // Condition 2: the items, from the obligations that state it: an
            // item without outcome, and under the complete policy one whose outcome needs
            // a reason it does not give.
            failures.extend(
                owed.of_kind(ObligationKind::ItemWithoutOutcome)
                    .chain(owed.of_kind(ObligationKind::OutcomeWithoutReason))
                    .cloned()
                    .map(Failure::Item),
            );
    
            // Condition 3: every block of the change, in a document that loaded
            // or not.
            let mut proposals = plan.unresolved_proposals(&impact.change);
            proposals.sort_by(|a, b| {
                (&a.location.path, a.location.line).cmp(&(&b.location.path, b.location.line))
            });
            let proposal_places: Vec<(String, Option<usize>)> = proposals
                .iter()
                .map(|owed| (owed.location.path.clone(), owed.location.line))
                .collect();
            failures.extend(proposals.into_iter().map(Failure::Proposal));
    
            // Condition 4: what `docs sync` would write or refuse.
            let analysis = generated::analyze(&plan);
            let mut faults: Vec<Finding> = analysis.refusals;
            faults.extend(analysis.repairs);
            faults.extend(analysis.invalid_index);
            failures.extend(faults.iter().cloned().map(Failure::Generated));
            failures.extend(
                analysis
                    .failures
                    .into_iter()
                    .map(|(file, reason)| Failure::Rewrite { file, reason }),
            );
    
            // Condition 6: the affected authored sections without a current
            // accepted assessment, from the obligations that state it.
            failures.extend(assessment_failures(&owed));
    
            // The repository as it will be once the writes are done, judged by
            // `check`.
            let (found, rewrite, unsynced) = Self::after_removal(opened, &record_path);
            for finding in found {
                let repeats = faults.contains(&finding)
                    || (finding.kind == kind::PROPOSED_MISSING_CHANGE
                        && proposal_places.iter().any(|(path, line)| {
                            *path == finding.file
                                && matches!(
                                    finding.location,
                                    Location::Line(at) if *line == Some(at)
                                )
                        }));
                if !repeats {
                    failures.push(Failure::AfterRemoval(finding));
                }
            }
            // A sync that fails with no finding to say why is an error and not a
            // pass.
            if failures.is_empty()
                && let Some(error) = unsynced
            {
                return Err(LifecycleError::Sync(error));
            }
            let rewrite = if failures.is_empty() {
                rewrite
            } else {
                SyncReport::default()
            };
    
            let mut runs: Vec<Binding> = impact
                .bindings
                .into_iter()
                .filter(|binding| matches!(binding.kind(), BindingKind::Test | BindingKind::Example))
                .collect();
            runs.sort_by(|a, b| {
                (a.capability(), a.kind(), a.path()).cmp(&(b.capability(), b.kind(), b.path()))
            });
            Ok(Evaluation {
                failures,
                runs,
                record: record_path,
                rewrite,
            })
        }
    
        /// The findings of `check` over the repository as it will be once the
        /// writes are done, and the rewrite that gets it there.
        ///
        /// The record is not shown and the files of the rewrite hold their new
        /// text, so the generated blocks the removal stales are current in the
        /// model that is judged, and a link inside one to the removed record is not
        /// a fault. When the sync cannot run the blocks stay as they are and the
        /// findings are those of the repository without the record, minus the
        /// blocks it stales; the error is returned with them.
        fn after_removal(
            opened: &Opened,
            record: &RelPath,
        ) -> (Vec<Finding>, SyncReport, Option<SyncError>) {
            let without = opened.workplan_after(record, &[]);
            match without.sync(&SyncOptions::default()) {
                Ok(report) => {
                    let findings = opened.workplan_after(record, &report.edits).check();
                    (findings, report, None)
                }
                Err(error) => {
                    let findings = without
                        .check()
                        .into_iter()
                        .filter(|finding| finding.kind != kind::GENERATED_STALE)
                        .collect();
                    (findings, SyncReport::default(), Some(error))
                }
            }
        }
    
        /// Condition 5: run every binding and return every one that did not pass.
        /// A failing example is also an `example-failing` obligation.
        fn run_bound(
            &self,
            opened: &Opened,
            runs: &[Binding],
        ) -> Result<(Vec<Failure>, Vec<Evidence>), LifecycleError> {
            let executor = Executor::new(opened.workspace(), &self.runner)?;
            let mut owed = Vec::new();
            let mut receipts = Vec::new();
            let mut tests = Vec::new();
            let mut examples = Vec::new();
            for binding in runs {
                let evidence: Evidence = executor.run(binding)?;
                let satisfied = matches!(
                    evidence.outcome(),
                    BoundOutcome::Passed | BoundOutcome::NotApplicable { .. }
                );
                receipts.push(evidence.clone());
                if satisfied {
                    continue;
                }
                if binding.kind() == BindingKind::Example {
                    owed.extend(examples::obligation(binding, &evidence).map(Failure::Owed));
                    examples.push(Failure::Example(evidence));
                } else {
                    tests.push(Failure::Test(evidence));
                }
            }
            owed.extend(tests);
            owed.extend(examples);
            Ok((owed, receipts))
        }
    
        /// Require that the record is a regular file directly in a `work/changes`
        /// that is a directory below a `work` that is a directory, neither a link,
        /// so the removal removes the record and no other file.
        fn judge_record(opened: &Opened, record: &RelPath, ran: bool) -> Result<(), LifecycleError> {
            let refused = |path: String, reason: &str| LifecycleError::RecordRefused {
                path,
                ran,
                source: io::Error::new(io::ErrorKind::InvalidInput, reason.to_string()),
            };
            match files::target(opened.dir(), DocType::Change.directory(), record) {
                Ok(Target {
                    kind: Some(EntryKind::File),
                    ..
                }) => Ok(()),
                Ok(_) => Err(refused(
                    record.as_str().to_string(),
                    "it is not a regular file",
                )),
                Err(error) => Err(LifecycleError::RecordRefused {
                    path: error.path,
                    ran,
                    source: error.source,
                }),
            }
        }
    
        /// The writes, in their fixed order: the generated blocks, and then, as the
        /// last step, the record.
        fn write(
            opened: &Opened,
            record: &RelPath,
            rewrite: &SyncReport,
            evidence: Vec<Evidence>,
        ) -> Result<Completion, LifecycleError> {
            let rewritten: Vec<String> = rewrite
                .edits
                .iter()
                .map(|(path, _)| path.as_str().to_string())
                .collect();
            if !rewrite.is_empty() {
                Workplan::write(rewrite, opened.dir())
                    .map_err(|source| LifecycleError::Rewrite { source })?;
            }
            fs::remove_file(opened.dir().join(record.as_str())).map_err(|source| {
                LifecycleError::Remove {
                    path: record.as_str().to_string(),
                    rewritten: rewritten.clone(),
                    source,
                }
            })?;
            Ok(Completion::Completed {
                removed: record.as_str().to_string(),
                rewritten,
                evidence,
            })
        }
    }
  in crates/raf-workplan/src/lifecycle/completion.rs
    // SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2026 Blackcat Informatics® Inc. <paudley@blackcatinformatics.ca>
    // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT OR Apache-2.0 OR MulanPSL-2.0
    
    //! What `complete` returns: the removal that happened, or every reason it did
    //! not.
    
    use std::collections::BTreeSet;
    use std::fmt;
    
    use raf_okf::Location;
    
    use crate::Finding;
    use crate::bound::Evidence;
    use crate::finding::kind;
    use crate::obligation::{Obligation, ObligationKind};
    
    use super::verification::evidence_line;
    
    /// The condition of the completion gate a [`Failure`] is a fault of.
    ///
    /// The six conditions of the Contract are numbered as it numbers them. The
    /// two others are not conditions of the list: the check of the repository as
    /// it will be once the record is gone, and the repository changing while the
    /// bound tests and examples ran.
    #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]
    #[non_exhaustive]
    pub enum Condition {
        /// Condition 1: the obligation list of `reconcile` is not empty.
        Obligations,
        /// Condition 2: an item has no valid `outcome`, or a `moved` outcome names
        /// no change record, or a `dropped` or `moved` item
        /// has no `reason`.
        Outcomes,
        /// Condition 3: a PROPOSED block of the change remains in a capability
        /// document.
        Proposals,
        /// Condition 4: `docs sync` would write something.
        GeneratedBlocks,
        /// Condition 5: a bound test or example neither passes nor has a proved
        /// platform exclusion.
        BoundRuns,
        /// Condition 6: an authored section in `affects` has no current
        /// assessment with the verdict `accepted`.
        Assessments,
        /// The repository without the record has a `check` finding.
        AfterRemoval,
        /// The repository changed between the bound runs and the writes.
        ChangedDuringRun,
    }
    
    impl Condition {
        /// The stable kebab-case name of the condition.
        #[must_use]
        pub const fn name(self) -> &'static str {
            match self {
                Self::Obligations => "obligations",
                Self::Outcomes => "outcomes",
                Self::Proposals => "proposals",
                Self::GeneratedBlocks => "generated-blocks",
                Self::BoundRuns => "bound-runs",
                Self::Assessments => "assessments",
                Self::AfterRemoval => "after-removal",
                Self::ChangedDuringRun => "changed-during-run",
            }
        }
    
        /// The number the Contract gives the condition, for the six of its list.
        #[must_use]
        pub const fn number(self) -> Option<u8> {
            match self {
                Self::Obligations => Some(1),
                Self::Outcomes => Some(2),
                Self::Proposals => Some(3),
                Self::GeneratedBlocks => Some(4),
                Self::BoundRuns => Some(5),
                Self::Assessments => Some(6),
                Self::AfterRemoval | Self::ChangedDuringRun => None,
            }
        }
    }
    
    /// One reason `complete` refuses.
    ///
    /// A fault that two conditions both state stands as one failure under each:
    /// an unresolved PROPOSED block is an obligation of condition 1 and a failure
    /// of condition 3, a stale block in a listed capability is an obligation and a
    /// failure of condition 4, an item without outcome, or a
    /// `dropped` or `moved` item without reason, is an obligation and a failure of
    /// condition 2, a failing bound example is an `example-failing`
    /// obligation and a failure of condition 5, and an affected section without a
    /// current accepted assessment is an `assessment-missing`,
    /// `assessment-stale` or `assessment-rejected` obligation and a failure of
    /// condition 6.
    #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    #[non_exhaustive]
    pub enum Failure {
        /// Condition 1: one obligation of the list, of any kind.
        Owed(Obligation),
        /// Condition 2: an item of the record with no valid outcome (an
        /// `item-without-outcome` obligation), or a `dropped`
        /// or `moved` item with no `reason` (an `outcome-without-reason`
        /// obligation).
        Item(Obligation),
        /// Condition 3: a PROPOSED block of the change that remains, in a document
        /// that loaded or not (an `unresolved-proposal` obligation).
        Proposal(Obligation),
        /// Condition 4: a generated block `docs sync` would rewrite, or a file it
        /// would refuse.
        Generated(Finding),
        /// Condition 4: the rewrite of a file does not read back as a document.
        Rewrite {
            /// The file.
            file: String,
            /// Why.
            reason: String,
        },
        /// Condition 5: a bound test that did not pass, or whose run proves
        /// nothing.
        Test(Evidence),
        /// Condition 5: a bound example or documentation test that did not pass.
        /// It is also an `example-failing` obligation, in [`Failure::Owed`].
        Example(Evidence),
        /// Condition 6: an authored section in `affects` with no current
        /// assessment whose verdict is `accepted` (an `assessment-missing`,
        /// `assessment-stale` or `assessment-rejected` obligation). A section has
        /// one for each such obligation, one `assessment-stale` for each input
        /// that moved, and the headline counts the section once.
        Assessment(Obligation),
        /// A finding of `check` over the repository without the record. The state
        /// rules are judged here: a capability that is not `delivered` and is
        /// listed by no other open record is `no-open-record`.
        AfterRemoval(Finding),
        /// The repository changed after the bound tests and examples ran, so what
        /// they proved is not what would be completed.
        Changed {
            /// What differs.
            what: String,
        },
    }
    
    impl Failure {
        /// The condition the failure is a fault of.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn condition(&self) -> Condition {
            match self {
                Self::Owed(_) => Condition::Obligations,
                Self::Item(_) => Condition::Outcomes,
                Self::Proposal(_) => Condition::Proposals,
                Self::Generated(_) | Self::Rewrite { .. } => Condition::GeneratedBlocks,
                Self::Test(_) | Self::Example(_) => Condition::BoundRuns,
                Self::Assessment(_) => Condition::Assessments,
                Self::AfterRemoval(_) => Condition::AfterRemoval,
                Self::Changed { .. } => Condition::ChangedDuringRun,
            }
        }
    
        /// The obligation the failure is, for conditions 1 to 3 and 6.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn obligation(&self) -> Option<&Obligation> {
            match self {
                Self::Owed(obligation)
                | Self::Item(obligation)
                | Self::Proposal(obligation)
                | Self::Assessment(obligation) => Some(obligation),
                _ => None,
            }
        }
    
        /// The kind of the obligation the failure is.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn obligation_kind(&self) -> Option<ObligationKind> {
            self.obligation().map(|obligation| obligation.kind)
        }
    
        /// The kind of the `check` finding the failure is, for the failures that
        /// are findings.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn finding_kind(&self) -> Option<&'static str> {
            match self {
                Self::Generated(finding) | Self::AfterRemoval(finding) => Some(finding.kind),
                _ => None,
            }
        }
    
        /// The stable kebab-case name of what failed: the kind of the obligation
        /// for conditions 1 to 3 and 6, the kind of the finding for a generated block or
        /// a finding once the record is removed, and `generated-rewrite`,
        /// `bound-test`, `bound-example` or `changed-during-run` for the others.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn name(&self) -> &'static str {
            match self {
                Self::Owed(obligation)
                | Self::Item(obligation)
                | Self::Proposal(obligation)
                | Self::Assessment(obligation) => obligation.kind.name(),
                Self::Generated(finding) | Self::AfterRemoval(finding) => finding.kind,
                Self::Rewrite { .. } => "generated-rewrite",
                Self::Test(_) => "bound-test",
                Self::Example(_) => "bound-example",
                Self::Changed { .. } => "changed-during-run",
            }
        }
    
        /// Whether the line of the failure repeats the line of another failure in
        /// `all`, so that `Display` of a [`Completion`] prints it once.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn is_repeated_in(&self, all: &[Failure]) -> bool {
            match self {
                Self::Item(_) | Self::Proposal(_) | Self::Example(_) | Self::Assessment(_) => true,
                Self::Generated(finding) => {
                    finding.kind == kind::GENERATED_STALE
                        && all.iter().filter_map(Failure::obligation).any(|owed| {
                            owed.kind == ObligationKind::GeneratedStale
                                && owed.location.path == finding.file
                                && matches!(
                                    finding.location,
                                    Location::Line(line) if owed.location.line == Some(line)
                                )
                        })
                }
                _ => false,
            }
        }
    }
    
    impl fmt::Display for Failure {
        /// One line, with no line ending.
        fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
            match self {
                Self::Owed(obligation)
                | Self::Item(obligation)
                | Self::Proposal(obligation)
                | Self::Assessment(obligation) => obligation.fmt(f),
                Self::Generated(finding) => finding.fmt(f),
                Self::Rewrite { file, reason } => write!(
                    f,
                    "{file}: the rewritten generated blocks do not read back as a document: {reason}"
                ),
                Self::Test(evidence) | Self::Example(evidence) => f.write_str(&evidence_line(evidence)),
                Self::AfterRemoval(finding) => {
                    write!(f, "once the record is removed, {finding}")
                }
                Self::Changed { what } => write!(
                    f,
                    "the repository changed after the bound tests and examples ran, so nothing was written: {what}"
                ),
            }
        }
    }
    
    /// How `change complete` ended when it could look at the repository.
    ///
    /// [`Completion::Refused`] is the exit code 1 of the command and
    /// [`Completion::Completed`] the exit code 0. `Display` is the text the
    /// command prints: for a refusal, `refused:` and a count for each kind of
    /// failure, then one line for each failure that no other line repeats; for a
    /// completion, `completed: removed <path>` and a line for each file whose
    /// generated blocks were rewritten.
    #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    #[non_exhaustive]
    pub enum Completion {
        /// The generated blocks the removal stales were rewritten and the record
        /// was removed, in that order, and nothing else was written.
        Completed {
            /// The repository-relative path of the record that was removed.
            removed: String,
            /// The repository-relative paths of the files whose generated blocks
            /// were rewritten, sorted.
            rewritten: Vec<String>,
            /// Actual binding outcomes, including justified host non-applicability.
            evidence: Vec<Evidence>,
        },
        /// Nothing was written. Every failure is listed, in the order of the
        /// conditions, and none is left out because another was found.
        Refused {
            /// The failures, never empty.
            failures: Vec<Failure>,
            /// Actual binding outcomes; empty when refused before binding execution.
            evidence: Vec<Evidence>,
        },
    }
    
    impl Completion {
        /// Whether the record was removed.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn is_completed(&self) -> bool {
            matches!(self, Self::Completed { .. })
        }
    
        /// The failures of a refusal; none for a completion.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn failures(&self) -> &[Failure] {
            match self {
                Self::Completed { .. } => &[],
                Self::Refused { failures, .. } => failures,
            }
        }
    
        /// Actual outcomes from the binding phase, without counting nonexecution as a pass.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn evidence(&self) -> &[Evidence] {
            match self {
                Self::Completed { evidence, .. } | Self::Refused { evidence, .. } => evidence,
            }
        }
    
        /// The failures of one condition.
        pub fn of_condition(&self, condition: Condition) -> impl Iterator<Item = &Failure> {
            self.failures()
                .iter()
                .filter(move |failure| failure.condition() == condition)
        }
    
        /// The obligations that stand as failures, of every kind.
        pub fn obligations(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &Obligation> {
            self.failures().iter().filter_map(|failure| match failure {
                Failure::Owed(obligation) => Some(obligation),
                _ => None,
            })
        }
    }
    
    /// `1 obligation` or `2 obligations`.
    fn counted(count: usize, one: &str, many: &str) -> Option<String> {
        match count {
            0 => None,
            1 => Some(format!("1 {one}")),
            _ => Some(format!("{count} {many}")),
        }
    }
    
    /// The sections that condition 6 refuses: one for each capability and
    /// section, however many `assessment-*` obligations it has (one
    /// `assessment-stale` for each input that moved).
    fn sections_without_assessment(failures: &[Failure]) -> usize {
        failures
            .iter()
            .filter_map(|failure| match failure {
                Failure::Assessment(owed) => Some((&owed.subject, owed.section)),
                _ => None,
            })
            .collect::<BTreeSet<_>>()
            .len()
    }
    
    /// The counts of a refusal, in the order of the conditions.
    fn summary(failures: &[Failure]) -> String {
        let count = |wanted: fn(&Failure) -> bool| failures.iter().filter(|f| wanted(f)).count();
        let stale = count(
            |f| matches!(f, Failure::Generated(finding) if finding.kind == kind::GENERATED_STALE),
        );
        let faulty = count(|f| matches!(f, Failure::Generated(_) | Failure::Rewrite { .. })) - stale;
        let parts = [
            counted(
                count(|f| matches!(f, Failure::Owed(_))),
                "obligation",
                "obligations",
            ),
            counted(
                count(
                    |f| matches!(f, Failure::Item(owed) if owed.kind != ObligationKind::OutcomeWithoutReason),
                ),
                "item without outcome",
                "items without outcome",
            ),
            counted(
                count(
                    |f| matches!(f, Failure::Item(owed) if owed.kind == ObligationKind::OutcomeWithoutReason),
                ),
                "item without a reason",
                "items without a reason",
            ),
            counted(
                count(|f| matches!(f, Failure::Proposal(_))),
                "unresolved PROPOSED block",
                "unresolved PROPOSED blocks",
            ),
            counted(stale, "stale generated block", "stale generated blocks"),
            counted(faulty, "generated block fault", "generated block faults"),
            counted(
                count(|f| matches!(f, Failure::Test(_))),
                "bound test failing",
                "bound tests failing",
            ),
            counted(
                count(|f| matches!(f, Failure::Example(_))),
                "bound example failing",
                "bound examples failing",
            ),
            counted(
                sections_without_assessment(failures),
                "section without a current accepted assessment",
                "sections without a current accepted assessment",
            ),
            counted(
                count(|f| matches!(f, Failure::AfterRemoval(_))),
                "finding once the record is removed",
                "findings once the record is removed",
            ),
            counted(
                count(|f| matches!(f, Failure::Changed { .. })),
                "change during the run",
                "changes during the run",
            ),
        ];
        parts.into_iter().flatten().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")
    }
    
    impl Completion {
        /// The first line of the text of the completion, without its line ending:
        /// `completed: removed <path>`, or `refused:` and the count of each kind
        /// of failure.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn headline(&self) -> String {
            match self {
                Self::Completed { removed, .. } => format!("completed: removed {removed}"),
                Self::Refused { failures, .. } => format!("refused: {}", summary(failures)),
            }
        }
    }
    
    impl fmt::Display for Completion {
        fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
            writeln!(f, "{}", self.headline())?;
            for evidence in self.evidence().iter().filter(|evidence| {
                matches!(
                    evidence.outcome(),
                    crate::bound::BoundOutcome::NotApplicable { .. }
                )
            }) {
                writeln!(f, "{}", evidence_line(evidence))?;
            }
            match self {
                Self::Completed { rewritten, .. } => {
                    for path in rewritten {
                        writeln!(f, "rewrote {path}")?;
                    }
                }
                Self::Refused { failures, .. } => {
                    for failure in failures
                        .iter()
                        .filter(|failure| !failure.is_repeated_in(failures))
                    {
                        writeln!(f, "{failure}")?;
                    }
                }
            }
            Ok(())
        }
    }
    
    #[cfg(test)]
    mod tests {
        use raf_okf::{CapabilitySection, Location};
    
        use super::{Completion, Condition, Failure};
        use crate::Finding;
        use crate::finding::kind;
        use crate::obligation::{
            Needed, Obligation, ObligationKind, OutcomeProblem, Part, Place, Subject, SyncProblem,
        };
    
        fn item(number: usize) -> Obligation {
            Obligation {
                kind: ObligationKind::ItemWithoutOutcome,
                subject: Subject::Change("change:shape".to_string()),
                section: Part::Item(number),
                location: Place {
                    path: "work/changes/shape.md".to_string(),
                    line: None,
                },
                evidence: Needed::Outcome(OutcomeProblem::Missing),
            }
        }
    
        fn stale(path: &str, line: usize) -> Obligation {
            Obligation {
                kind: ObligationKind::GeneratedStale,
                subject: Subject::Index,
                section: Part::Document,
                location: Place {
                    path: path.to_string(),
                    line: Some(line),
                },
                evidence: Needed::SyncRun(SyncProblem::Stale("catalogue".to_string())),
            }
        }
    
        fn finding(kind: &'static str, file: &str, line: usize) -> Finding {
            Finding::new(kind, file, Location::Line(line), "a message", "a fix")
        }
    
        #[test]
        fn a_completion_prints_the_removal_and_each_rewritten_file() {
            let done = Completion::Completed {
                evidence: Vec::new(),
                removed: "work/changes/shape.md".to_string(),
                rewritten: Vec::new(),
            };
            assert_eq!(
                done.to_string(),
                "completed: removed work/changes/shape.md\n"
            );
            assert!(done.is_completed());
            assert_eq!(done.failures(), []);
            let done = Completion::Completed {
                evidence: Vec::new(),
                removed: "work/changes/shape.md".to_string(),
                rewritten: vec!["a.md".to_string(), "b.md".to_string()],
            };
            assert_eq!(
                done.to_string(),
                "completed: removed work/changes/shape.md\nrewrote a.md\nrewrote b.md\n"
            );
        }
    
        #[test]
        fn the_counts_of_a_refusal_are_singular_or_plural_and_in_the_order_of_the_conditions() {
            let refused = Completion::Refused {
                evidence: Vec::new(),
                failures: vec![
                    Failure::Owed(item(1)),
                    Failure::Owed(item(2)),
                    Failure::Item(item(1)),
                    Failure::Item(item(2)),
                    Failure::Generated(finding(kind::GENERATED_STALE, "a.md", 3)),
                    Failure::Generated(finding(kind::GENERATED_UNKNOWN_KIND, "b.md", 4)),
                    Failure::Rewrite {
                        file: "c.md".to_string(),
                        reason: "too long".to_string(),
                    },
                    Failure::AfterRemoval(finding(kind::NO_OPEN_RECORD, "d.md", 1)),
                    Failure::Changed {
                        what: "it moved".to_string(),
                    },
                ],
            };
            let text = refused.to_string();
            assert_eq!(
                text.lines().next(),
                Some(
                    "refused: 2 obligations, 2 items without outcome, 1 stale generated block, 2 generated block faults, 1 finding once the record is removed, 1 change during the run"
                )
            );
            // The obligations print once each, the repeats not at all.
            assert_eq!(text.matches("item-without-outcome").count(), 2, "{text}");
            assert_eq!(refused.failures().len(), 9);
            assert_eq!(refused.of_condition(Condition::Outcomes).count(), 2);
            assert_eq!(refused.obligations().count(), 2);
        }
    
        #[test]
        fn a_stale_block_prints_a_line_unless_an_obligation_already_does() {
            let owed = Failure::Owed(stale("docs/capabilities/index.md", 7));
            let same = Failure::Generated(finding(
                kind::GENERATED_STALE,
                "docs/capabilities/index.md",
                7,
            ));
            let elsewhere = Failure::Generated(finding(
                kind::GENERATED_STALE,
                "docs/capabilities/other.md",
                7,
            ));
            let other_line = Failure::Generated(finding(
                kind::GENERATED_STALE,
                "docs/capabilities/index.md",
                9,
            ));
            let refused = Completion::Refused {
                evidence: Vec::new(),
                failures: vec![owed, same, elsewhere, other_line],
            };
            let text = refused.to_string();
            assert_eq!(
                text.lines().count(),
                4,
                "the counts, the obligation and two blocks: {text}"
            );
            assert!(text.contains("other.md: line 7"), "{text}");
            assert!(text.contains("index.md: line 9"), "{text}");
            assert!(!text.contains("index.md: line 7"), "{text}");
        }
    
        #[test]
        fn a_section_without_an_accepted_assessment_counts_under_condition_six_and_prints_once() {
            let missing = |section| Obligation {
                kind: ObligationKind::AssessmentMissing,
                subject: Subject::Capability("cap:shape".to_string()),
                section: Part::Section(section),
                location: Place {
                    path: "docs/capabilities/shape.md".to_string(),
                    line: None,
                },
                evidence: Needed::Assessed {
                    subject: "cap:shape#usage".to_string(),
                    assessment: "assessment:shape-usage".to_string(),
                },
            };
            let usage = missing(CapabilitySection::Usage);
            let contract = missing(CapabilitySection::Contract);
            let refused = Completion::Refused {
                evidence: Vec::new(),
                failures: vec![
                    Failure::Owed(contract.clone()),
                    Failure::Owed(usage.clone()),
                    Failure::Assessment(contract),
                    Failure::Assessment(usage),
                ],
            };
            let text = refused.to_string();
            assert_eq!(
                text.lines().next(),
                Some("refused: 2 obligations, 2 sections without a current accepted assessment")
            );
            assert_eq!(text.lines().count(), 3, "{text}");
            assert_eq!(refused.of_condition(Condition::Assessments).count(), 2);
            let one = Completion::Refused {
                evidence: Vec::new(),
                failures: vec![Failure::Assessment(missing(CapabilitySection::Usage))],
            };
            assert_eq!(
                one.headline(),
                "refused: 1 section without a current accepted assessment"
            );
            assert_eq!(one.failures()[0].name(), "assessment-missing");
            assert_eq!(one.failures()[0].condition().number(), Some(6));
        }
    
        #[test]
        fn a_section_with_three_moved_inputs_is_one_section_in_the_headline() {
            let stale = |section, input: &str| Obligation {
                kind: ObligationKind::AssessmentStale,
                subject: Subject::Capability("cap:shape".to_string()),
                section: Part::Section(section),
                location: Place {
                    path: "docs/capabilities/shape.md".to_string(),
                    line: None,
                },
                evidence: Needed::Assessed {
                    subject: "cap:shape#usage".to_string(),
                    assessment: format!("assessment:shape-usage ({input} moved)"),
                },
            };
            let refusal = |obligations: Vec<Obligation>| Completion::Refused {
                evidence: Vec::new(),
                failures: obligations
                    .iter()
                    .cloned()
                    .map(Failure::Owed)
                    .chain(obligations.iter().cloned().map(Failure::Assessment))
                    .collect(),
            };
            // One section, three moved inputs: three obligations and lines, one
            // section.
            let one = refusal(vec![
                stale(CapabilitySection::Usage, "section"),
                stale(CapabilitySection::Usage, "shape::push"),
                stale(CapabilitySection::Usage, "shape::finish"),
            ]);
            assert_eq!(
                one.headline(),
                "refused: 3 obligations, 1 section without a current accepted assessment"
            );
            assert_eq!(one.to_string().lines().count(), 4, "{one}");
            assert_eq!(one.of_condition(Condition::Assessments).count(), 3);
            // The neighbour: two sections, one moved input each.
            let two = refusal(vec![
                stale(CapabilitySection::Usage, "section"),
                stale(CapabilitySection::Contract, "section"),
            ]);
            assert_eq!(
                two.headline(),
                "refused: 2 obligations, 2 sections without a current accepted assessment"
            );
            // The same section of another capability is another section.
            let mut other = stale(CapabilitySection::Usage, "section");
            other.subject = Subject::Capability("cap:other".to_string());
            let apart = refusal(vec![stale(CapabilitySection::Usage, "section"), other]);
            assert_eq!(
                apart.headline(),
                "refused: 2 obligations, 2 sections without a current accepted assessment"
            );
        }
    
        #[test]
        fn conditions_are_numbered_as_the_contract_numbers_them() {
            let numbers: Vec<Option<u8>> = [
                Condition::Obligations,
                Condition::Outcomes,
                Condition::Proposals,
                Condition::GeneratedBlocks,
                Condition::BoundRuns,
                Condition::Assessments,
                Condition::AfterRemoval,
                Condition::ChangedDuringRun,
            ]
            .into_iter()
            .map(Condition::number)
            .collect();
            assert_eq!(
                numbers,
                [
                    Some(1),
                    Some(2),
                    Some(3),
                    Some(4),
                    Some(5),
                    Some(6),
                    None,
                    None
                ]
            );
        }
    }

    // SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2026 Blackcat Informatics® Inc. <paudley@blackcatinformatics.ca>
