    /// Which version of a document a [`LifecycleError::Document`] is about.
    #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
    #[non_exhaustive]
    pub enum Side {
        /// The file as it was at the merge base.
        MergeBase,
        /// The file as it is in the working tree.
        WorkingTree,
    }
    
    impl fmt::Display for Side {
        fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
            f.write_str(match self {
                Self::MergeBase => "at the merge base",
                Self::WorkingTree => "in the working tree",
            })
        }
    }
    
    /// Why a command that starts a process cannot give its answer. Every case is
    /// an error and none is a pass: the tool could not look, which is not the
    /// same as finding nothing. A command-line wrapper exits with 2 for each.
    #[derive(Debug)]
    #[non_exhaustive]
    pub enum LifecycleError {
        /// A `git` question has no answer: the Git is older than 2.30, the
        /// repository has no default branch (`git remote set-head origin --auto`
        /// sets it), the history has no merge base, a file at the merge base is
        /// over the bound, or `git` could not run.
        Git(GitError),
        /// The repository cannot be opened.
        Gate(GateError),
        /// The slug names no loaded change record.
        Change(ChangeLookupError),
        /// The change cannot be reconciled: a capability it lists did not load.
        Reconcile(ReconcileError),
        /// A process of a bound test or example could not be started or read to
        /// its end, so the run has no verdict.
        Exec(ExecError),
        /// The source index cannot say which functions are tests.
        Index(LoadError),
        /// The public surface of a library or binary target that a `code` binding
        /// reaches cannot be listed, so which bound public items a change with
        /// `contract_effect: none` touches is unknown (the complete policy).
        Surface {
            /// The package of the target.
            package: String,
            /// The crate path of the target.
            target: String,
            /// Which source index: the working tree's, or the one loaded at the
            /// merge base.
            side: Side,
            /// Why the source index cannot list it.
            reason: String,
        },
        /// A capability document cannot be compared: it is not readable, it is
        /// not UTF-8, it does not parse as a document body, or it has no such
        /// section.
        Document {
            /// The repository-relative path of the file.
            path: String,
            /// Which version of the file.
            side: Side,
            /// Why it cannot be compared.
            reason: String,
        },
        /// A command over every capability found capability documents that did
        /// not load, so what they bind is unknown.
        Unloaded {
            /// The ids of the capabilities, sorted.
            capabilities: Vec<String>,
            /// The findings about their files.
            findings: Vec<Finding>,
        },
        /// The generated blocks that the removal of a record stales cannot be
        /// computed, although `check` of the repository without the record is
        /// clean. Nothing was written.
        Sync(SyncError),
        /// The rewrite of the generated blocks failed, so the record was not
        /// removed. The files are written one at a time through a temporary file
        /// and a rename, so some of them may already hold their new text.
        Rewrite {
            /// Why the write failed.
            source: io::Error,
        },
        /// The record could not be removed, after the generated blocks were
        /// rewritten. The blocks no longer list the record.
        Remove {
            /// The repository-relative path of the record.
            path: String,
            /// The files whose generated blocks were rewritten.
            rewritten: Vec<String>,
            /// Why the removal failed.
            source: io::Error,
        },
        /// The way to the record is refused: `work` or `work/changes` is a
        /// symbolic link or not a directory, or the record is not a regular file
        /// there, so removing it could remove another file. Judged with each
        /// judgement of the static conditions: the first before anything runs,
        /// the second after the bound tests and examples ran and before the
        /// writes. Nothing was written in either case.
        RecordRefused {
            /// The refused directory, or the path of the record.
            path: String,
            /// Whether the bound tests and examples had run: `false` when the
            /// first judgement refused, `true` when the second did.
            ran: bool,
            /// Why it is refused.
            source: io::Error,
        },
    }
    
    impl fmt::Display for LifecycleError {
        fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
            match self {
                Self::Git(error) => error.fmt(f),
                Self::Gate(error) => error.fmt(f),
                Self::Change(error) => error.fmt(f),
                Self::Reconcile(error) => error.fmt(f),
                Self::Exec(error) => error.fmt(f),
                Self::Index(error) => write!(f, "the source index cannot list the tests: {error}"),
                Self::Surface {
                    package,
                    target,
                    side,
                    reason,
                } => write!(
                    f,
                    "the public surface of `{target}` in the package `{package}` {side} cannot be listed, so the bound public items the change touches are unknown: {reason}"
                ),
                Self::Document { path, side, reason } => write!(
                    f,
                    "`{path}` {side} cannot be compared: {reason}; a section that cannot be read is not reported as touched or untouched"
                ),
                Self::Unloaded {
                    capabilities,
                    findings,
                } => {
                    write!(
                        f,
                        "{} did not load, so what {} bind is unknown; fix the findings first",
                        capabilities.join(", "),
                        if capabilities.len() == 1 {
                            "it"
                        } else {
                            "they"
                        }
                    )?;
                    write_findings(f, findings)
                }
                Self::Sync(error) => write!(
                    f,
                    "the generated blocks that removing the record stales cannot be computed: {error}; nothing was written"
                ),
                Self::Rewrite { source } => write!(
                    f,
                    "the generated blocks could not be rewritten: {source}; the record was not removed; some files may already hold their new text, which `docs sync` finishes or restores"
                ),
                Self::Remove {
                    path,
                    rewritten,
                    source,
                } => write!(
                    f,
                    "`{path}` could not be removed: {source}; the generated blocks of {} were already rewritten and no longer list it, so remove the file yourself, or run `docs sync` and then `change complete` again",
                    if rewritten.is_empty() {
                        "no file".to_string()
                    } else {
                        rewritten.join(", ")
                    }
                ),
                Self::RecordRefused { path, ran, source } => write!(
                    f,
                    "`{path}` is refused on the way to the change record at the {}: {source}; nothing was written, so make `work` and `work/changes` plain directories that hold the record",
                    if *ran {
                        "second judgement, after the bound tests and examples ran"
                    } else {
                        "first judgement, before anything ran"
                    }
                ),
            }
        }
    }
    
    impl Error for LifecycleError {
        fn source(&self) -> Option<&(dyn Error + 'static)> {
            match self {
                Self::Git(error) => Some(error),
                Self::Gate(error) => Some(error),
                Self::Change(error) => Some(error),
                Self::Reconcile(error) => Some(error),
                Self::Exec(error) => Some(error),
                Self::Index(error) => Some(error),
                Self::Sync(error) => Some(error),
                Self::Rewrite { source }
                | Self::Remove { source, .. }
                | Self::RecordRefused { source, .. } => Some(source),
                Self::Document { .. } | Self::Unloaded { .. } | Self::Surface { .. } => None,
            }
        }
    }
    
    impl From<GitError> for LifecycleError {
        fn from(error: GitError) -> Self {
            Self::Git(error)
        }
    }
    
    impl From<GateError> for LifecycleError {
        fn from(error: GateError) -> Self {
            Self::Gate(error)
        }
    }
    
    impl From<ChangeLookupError> for LifecycleError {
        fn from(error: ChangeLookupError) -> Self {
            Self::Change(error)
        }
    }
    
    impl From<ReconcileError> for LifecycleError {
        fn from(error: ReconcileError) -> Self {
            Self::Reconcile(error)
        }
    }
    
    impl From<ExecError> for LifecycleError {
        fn from(error: ExecError) -> Self {
            Self::Exec(error)
        }
    }
    
    impl From<SyncError> for LifecycleError {
        fn from(error: SyncError) -> Self {
            Self::Sync(error)
        }
    }
    
    impl From<LoadError> for LifecycleError {
        fn from(error: LoadError) -> Self {
            Self::Index(error)
        }
    }
    
    /// A repository opened for the commands that start a process: `reconcile`,
    /// `complete` and, in the same entry point, `verify`.
    ///
    /// [`Lifecycle::open`] asks `git --version` first and before any other
    /// process, and refuses a Git older than 2.30 (the first release whose
    /// `rev-parse` handles `--end-of-options`); only then does it open the
    /// repository ([`Gate::open`]). The version gate belongs to this entry point
    /// and not to [`Gate::open`], and the commands that need no Git (`change new`,
    /// `change impact` and [`verify`](super::verify)) do not go through it.
    ///
    /// Every `git` and `cargo` process is started by the [`Runner`] with the
    /// working directory of its [`Programs`], which is the root of the repository.
    /// `git` names a file at a commit from the top of the work tree, so the open also
    /// asks `git rev-parse --show-prefix` once: a workspace below the top of its
    /// repository (a monorepo) reads its documents at that prefix, and a prefix that
    /// the source-path pattern cannot carry is an error. A linked work tree is its
    /// own top. The root must be inside a git work tree.
    ///
    /// [`Lifecycle::complete`] opens the repository a second time, with the
    /// [`Gate`] it was opened with, to judge it again before it writes.
    ///
    /// The complete repository policy judges both reconciliation and the final
    /// completion check after the change record is removed.
    ///
    /// ```rust,no_run
    /// use raf_workplan::exec::Programs;
    /// use raf_workplan::lifecycle::Lifecycle;
    ///
    /// let lifecycle = Lifecycle::open(Programs::new("."))?;
    /// let owed = lifecycle.reconcile("incremental-parsing")?;
    /// print!("{owed}");
    /// std::process::exit(i32::from(!owed.is_empty()));
    /// # Ok::<(), raf_workplan::lifecycle::LifecycleError>(())
    /// ```
    #[derive(Debug)]
    pub struct Lifecycle<R = SystemRunner> {
        pub(super) gate: Gate,
        pub(super) opened: Opened,
        pub(super) runner: R,
        pub(super) prefix: Prefix,
    }
    
    impl Lifecycle<SystemRunner> {
        /// Open the repository in the working directory of `programs`, starting
        /// its `git` and `cargo`.
        ///
        /// # Errors
        ///
        /// [`LifecycleError::Git`] when the Git is older than 2.30, cannot be
        /// started or does not state a version (nothing else has run), or when the
        /// root is not inside a git work tree or below a directory the source-path
        /// pattern cannot carry;
        /// [`LifecycleError::Gate`] when the repository cannot be opened.
        pub fn open(programs: Programs) -> Result<Self, LifecycleError> {
            let gate = Gate::workspace(&programs.cwd).programs(programs.clone());
            Self::open_with(&gate, SystemRunner::new(programs))
        }
    }
    
    impl<R: Runner> Lifecycle<R> {
        /// Open the repository `gate` names, asking `runner` for every process of
        /// the commands. The runner's working directory must be the gate's, which
        /// [`Lifecycle::open`] guarantees.
        ///
        /// # Errors
        ///
        /// As [`Lifecycle::open`].
        pub fn open_with(gate: &Gate, runner: R) -> Result<Self, LifecycleError> {
            git::require_version(&runner)?;
            let prefix = git::show_prefix(&runner)?;
            let opened = gate.open()?;
            Ok(Self {
                gate: gate.clone(),
                opened,
                runner,
                prefix,
            })
        }
    
        /// The repository, as it was found at the open.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn opened(&self) -> &Opened {
            &self.opened
        }
    
        /// Where the root is below the top of its git work tree.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn prefix(&self) -> &Prefix {
            &self.prefix
        }
    
        /// The runner every process goes through.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn runner(&self) -> &R {
            &self.runner
        }
    
        /// The full obligation list of the change `change`: the static kinds of
        /// [`Workplan::reconcile`](crate::Workplan::reconcile) and the three that
        /// need a process.
        ///
        /// * `section-untouched`: for each section in `affects`, the section at
        ///   the merge base of `HEAD` and the default branch is compared with the
        ///   working tree ([`section_digest`](super::section_digest)); a section
        ///   with the same authored text is owed. `contract_effect: none` leaves
        ///   out `contract`, `usage` and `examples`. A repository with no default
        ///   branch is an error, so is a merge-base file that does not parse.
        /// * `evidence-changed`: each evidence record a claim of a listed
        ///   capability names is read at the same merge base; one that passed
        ///   there and now fails, holds a value over a limit (its own or the merge
        ///   base's), has lost a limit of the merge base, or no longer loads is
        ///   owed at each claim that names it. A record there that does not read
        ///   as an evidence record is an error.
        /// * `example-failing`: the example targets and documentation tests that
        ///   the capabilities of the change bind and that resolve are built and
        ///   run ([`Executor`]); one that fails, runs no test or proves nothing is
        ///   owed. Test bindings are not run here, and the examples of a binding
        ///   that does not resolve are not either (it is `binding-missing`).
        ///
        /// The merge base is asked for once, and only when a section or a claim
        /// needs it. The documents are loaded as they are on disk at the call.
        /// Nothing in the repository is written.
        ///
        /// # Errors
        ///
        /// [`LifecycleError::Change`] and [`LifecycleError::Reconcile`] as for
        /// [`Workplan::reconcile`](crate::Workplan::reconcile);
        /// [`LifecycleError::Git`] and [`LifecycleError::Document`] when a section
        /// or a record cannot be compared; [`LifecycleError::Exec`] when a process
        /// of an example cannot be started or read to its end.
        pub fn reconcile(&self, change: &str) -> Result<Reconciled, LifecycleError> {
            let plan = self.opened.workplan();
            let fixed = plan.reconcile(change)?;
            let impact = plan.impact(change)?;
            let effect = plan.change(change)?.value().contract_effect;
            let mut owed = fixed.obligations().to_vec();
            owed.extend(self.process_obligations(
                &self.opened,
                &plan,
                &impact,
                effect,
                true,
                &mut None,
            )?);
            Ok(Reconciled::new(fixed.change().to_string(), owed))
        }
    
        /// The obligations of a change that need a process, over `opened` and
        /// `plan`, its documents: `section-untouched`, `evidence-changed` and,
        /// under the complete policy for a change with `contract_effect: none`,
        /// `contract-effect-understated` from `git` at one merge base, and, when
        /// `examples` is set, `example-failing` from the executor. It is the one
        /// place [`Lifecycle::reconcile`] and
        /// [`Lifecycle::complete`](Lifecycle::complete) (which judges the examples
        /// by running them under condition 5) assemble them.
        pub(super) fn process_obligations(
            &self,
            opened: &Opened,
            plan: &Workplan<'_>,
            impact: &Impact,
            effect: ContractEffect,
            examples: bool,
            kept: &mut Option<touched::BaseIndex>,
        ) -> Result<Vec<Obligation>, LifecycleError> {
            let mut base = None;
            let mut owed = untouched::raise(
                opened,
                &self.runner,
                &self.prefix,
                impact,
                effect,
                &mut base,
            )?;
            owed.extend(changed::raise(
                opened,
                &self.runner,
                &self.prefix,
                impact,
                &mut base,
            )?);
            if effect == ContractEffect::None {
                owed.extend(touched::raise(
                    plan,
                    &self.runner,
                    &self.prefix,
                    &impact.change,
                    &mut base,
                    kept,
                )?);
            }
            if examples {
                let executor = Executor::new(opened.workspace(), &self.runner)?;
                owed.extend(examples::raise(&executor, impact)?);
            }
            Ok(owed)
        }
    
        /// Run the bound tests and examples of the change `change`, or of every
        /// capability when `change` is `None`. See [`verify`](super::verify).
        ///
        /// # Errors
        ///
        /// As [`verify`](super::verify).
        pub fn verify(&self, change: Option<&str>) -> Result<VerifyReport, LifecycleError> {
            verify(&self.opened, &self.runner, change)
        }
    }
  in crates/raf-workplan/src/lifecycle/tests.rs
    // SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2026 Blackcat Informatics® Inc. <paudley@blackcatinformatics.ca>
    // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT OR Apache-2.0 OR MulanPSL-2.0
    
    //! The `example-failing` mapping from a run to an obligation, over scripted
    //! answers: every way a run can end that real `cargo` fixtures cannot make
    //! quickly (a kill at the time limit, a kill for output) and the arguments of
    //! the one process an example is.
    
    use std::fmt::Write as _;
    use std::time::Duration;
    
    use raf_source::{MemoryTree, Workspace};
    
    use crate::Workplan;
    use crate::bound::Executor;
    use crate::exec::{ExecError, FakeRunner, Output, Program, Stream};
    use crate::obligation::{Needed, ObligationKind, Part, RunProblem, Subject};
    
    use super::{LifecycleError, examples};
    
    const RUN: [&str; 4] = ["run", "--frozen", "--package=demo", "--example=show"];
    
    fn workspace() -> Workspace {
        let tree = MemoryTree::from([
            (
                "Cargo.toml",
                "[package]\nname = \"demo\"\nversion = \"0.1.0\"\n",
            ),
            (
                "src/lib.rs",
                "/// Documented, no example.\npub fn bare() {}\n",
            ),
            ("examples/show.rs", "fn main() {}\n"),
        ]);
        Workspace::load(&tree, &["."]).expect("the workspace loads")
    }
    
    fn documents() -> MemoryTree {
        let mut sections = String::new();
        for name in [
            "Purpose",
            "Contract",
            "Usage",
            "Examples",
            "Constraints",
            "Explanation",
            "Verification",
        ] {
            write!(sections, "## {name}\n\nText.\n\n").expect("a string takes text");
        }
        let capability = format!(
            "---\ntype: Capability\nid: cap:demo\ntitle: Demo\ndescription: Demo.\n\
             state: implementing\nbindings:\n  code: []\n  tests: []\n  \
             examples: [demo::example_crate::show, demo::bare]\ndepends: []\n---\n\n# Demo\n\n{sections}"
        );
        let change = "---\ntype: Change\nid: change:shape\ntitle: Shape\ndescription: Shapes it.\n\
                      changes: [cap:demo]\naffects: []\ncontract_effect: none\nitems: []\n---\n\n\
                      ## Summary\n\nShapes.\n";
        MemoryTree::from([
            ("docs/capabilities/demo.md", capability.as_str()),
            ("work/changes/shape.md", change),
        ])
    }
    
    /// The obligations for the example `demo::example_crate::show` run with this
    /// answer, and the answers for the list pass of `bare`.
    fn raised(
        answer: Result<Output, ExecError>,
    ) -> Result<Vec<crate::obligation::Obligation>, LifecycleError> {
        let source = workspace();
        let docs = documents();
        let plan = Workplan::load(&docs, &source);
        let impact = plan.impact("shape").expect("the change loads");
        let argv: Vec<&str> = RUN
            .iter()
            .copied()
            .filter(|word| !word.is_empty())
            .collect();
        let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(Program::Cargo, &argv, answer).on(
            Program::Cargo,
            &[
                "test",
                "--frozen",
                "--package=demo",
                "--doc",
                "--",
                "--list",
            ],
            Ok(Output::new(Some(0), Vec::new(), Vec::new())),
        );
        let executor = Executor::new(&source, &runner).expect("the index lists its tests");
        examples::raise(&executor, &impact)
    }
    
    fn exited(code: Option<i32>) -> Output {
        Output::new(code, Vec::new(), b"error: whatever\n".to_vec())
    }
    
    /// The problem of the one example, ignoring the documented item with no
    /// documentation test, which is owed in every case.
    fn problem(answer: Result<Output, ExecError>) -> Option<RunProblem> {
        let owed = raised(answer).expect("the runs have verdicts");
        owed.into_iter()
            .find(|o| matches!(&o.evidence, Needed::PassingRun { binding, .. } if binding == "demo::example_crate::show"))
            .map(|o| match o.evidence {
                Needed::PassingRun { problem, .. } => problem,
                other => panic!("{other:?}"),
            })
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn every_way_a_run_can_fail_is_a_problem_and_a_pass_is_none() {
        assert_eq!(problem(Ok(exited(Some(0)))), None);
        assert_eq!(
            problem(Ok(exited(Some(101)))),
            Some(RunProblem::Fails { code: Some(101) })
        );
        assert_eq!(
            problem(Ok(exited(None))),
            Some(RunProblem::Fails { code: None })
        );
        assert_eq!(
            problem(Err(ExecError::Timeout {
                command: "cargo run".to_string(),
                timeout: Duration::from_secs(300),
                elapsed: Duration::from_secs(300),
            })),
            Some(RunProblem::TimedOut)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            problem(Err(ExecError::OutputTooLarge {
                command: "cargo run".to_string(),
                stream: Stream::Stdout,
                limit: 1,
                elapsed: Duration::from_millis(40),
            })),
            Some(RunProblem::Overflowed)
        );
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn an_example_that_cannot_be_started_is_an_error_and_has_no_problem() {
        let error = raised(Err(ExecError::Spawn {
            command: "cargo run".to_string(),
            program: "cargo".to_string(),
            kind: std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound,
            message: "No such file or directory".to_string(),
        }))
        .expect_err("no verdict");
        assert!(matches!(
            error,
            LifecycleError::Exec(ExecError::Spawn { .. })
        ));
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn the_obligation_names_the_capability_the_examples_section_and_the_binding() {
        let owed = raised(Ok(exited(Some(101)))).expect("verdicts");
        // The example, and the documented item with no documentation test.
        assert_eq!(owed.len(), 2);
        for obligation in &owed {
            assert_eq!(obligation.kind, ObligationKind::ExampleFailing);
            assert_eq!(
                obligation.subject,
                Subject::Capability("cap:demo".to_string())
            );
            assert_eq!(
                obligation.section,
                Part::Section(raf_okf::CapabilitySection::Examples)
            );
            assert_eq!(obligation.location.path, "docs/capabilities/demo.md");
            assert_eq!(obligation.location.line, None);
        }
        let lines: Vec<String> = owed.iter().map(ToString::to_string).collect();
        assert!(
            lines[0].contains("(`demo::bare` proves nothing: no documentation test of the package `demo` is listed for the item `bare`"),
            "{}",
            lines[0]
        );
        assert!(
            lines[1].ends_with("(`demo::example_crate::show` fails to build or run (exit code 101))"),
            "{}",
            lines[1]
        );
    }
  in crates/raf-workplan/src/lifecycle/touched.rs
    // SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2026 Blackcat Informatics® Inc. <paudley@blackcatinformatics.ca>
    // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT OR Apache-2.0 OR MulanPSL-2.0
    
    //! `contract-effect-understated`: under the complete policy, a change that says
    //! `contract_effect: none` and touches a bound public item.
    //!
    //! The public surface of a side is read from a source index: the working
    //! tree's, and one loaded at the merge base from its files. On each side the
    //! units are the items of the public surface of every library and binary
    //! target of a package that holds a bound item ([`Workspace::exported_items`]:
    //! the items another crate can name, the `pub` members of the inherent impls
    //! of an exported type and the members of an exported trait), and the
    //! members of the trait impls of an exported struct, enum or union. A unit is
    //! the same unit on both sides when its path, its kind and, for a member of an
    //! impl block, the tokens of the impl's header (its attributes, generics,
    //! trait, self type and where clause) are the same. It is bound when a `code`
    //! binding of any loaded capability covers it, itself or as an item below it,
    //! as two-way coverage reads it ([`covering`], [`covering_path`]).
    //!
    //! "Touch" is read literally. A unit is compared with its own counterpart as
    //! the `proc_macro2` token stream of its span: a doc comment arrives as a
    //! `#[doc]` attribute and counts, while whitespace and comments that are not
    //! doc comments are not tokens and do not; CRLF line endings read as LF, as
    //! `rustc` reads them. Then:
    //!
    //! * a bound unit whose counterpart has another stream is [`Touch::Tokens`];
    //! * a bound unit with no counterpart is [`Touch::New`]: new, made public,
    //!   given a new path, or under a changed impl header;
    //! * a bound unit of the merge base with no counterpart is
    //!   [`Touch::Hidden`] when the working tree still has an item of its path and
    //!   kind, off the surface, and [`Touch::Removed`] otherwise;
    //! * a re-export path of the surface that names another unit than at the
    //!   merge base is [`Touch::Retargeted`], one that is new is [`Touch::New`]
    //!   and one that is gone is [`Touch::Removed`].
    //!
    //! A path is reported once, by the first of these that holds.
    //!
    //! The source index at the merge base is loaded from the directories of the
    //! packages that hold a bound item, through a [`Tree`] of the files of the
    //! merge base: `git ls-tree -r -z --full-tree <merge-base>` lists them once,
    //! and each file the load reads is read once with the row
    //! `git cat-file blob <merge-base>:<prefix><path>` that `section-untouched`
    //! uses, within the 1 MiB bound of a blob. [`BaseIndex`] keeps that index, so
    //! the two judgements of `complete` at one merge base read it once.
    //!
    //! A merge base whose index does not load (a file that is not UTF-8 or does
    //! not parse, a module that is missing) is an error and not a pass, and so is
    //! a surface that a source index cannot list.
    
    use std::cell::RefCell;
    use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};
    use std::io;
    use std::ops::Range;
    
    use proc_macro2::{Delimiter, LineColumn, Spacing, TokenStream, TokenTree};
    use raf_source::syn::Item;
    use raf_source::syn::spanned::Spanned;
    use raf_source::{
        Entry, EntryKind, FileId, LoadError, Opened as TreeRead, RelPath, SelfType, SymbolKind,
        TargetKind, TestRole, Tree, TreeError, Workspace, parse_source,
    };
    
    use crate::Workplan;
    use crate::coverage::{covering, covering_path};
    use crate::exec::Runner;
    use crate::git::{self, MergeBase, Prefix, TreeEntry};
    use crate::graph::{Binding, BindingKind};
    use crate::obligation::{Needed, Obligation, ObligationKind, Part, Place, Subject, Touch, Touched};
    
    use super::session::{LifecycleError, Side};
    use super::untouched::{blob, merge_base};
    
    /// The source index loaded at one merge base from the directories of the
    /// packages that hold a bound item, kept between two judgements of one
    /// change so it is read once: the files at a commit do not change.
    #[derive(Debug)]
    pub(super) struct BaseIndex {
        /// The commit id of the merge base.
        commit: String,
        /// The directories it was loaded from, sorted.
        roots: Vec<String>,
        /// The index, or `None` when none of the directories is at the merge
        /// base.
        workspace: Option<Workspace>,
    }
    
    /// The `contract-effect-understated` obligations of the change `change`, one
    /// for each touched bound public item, on the change record.
    ///
    /// The caller asks only under the complete policy and for a change with
    /// `contract_effect: none`. Nothing is asked of `git` when no `code` binding
    /// resolves to an item; otherwise the merge base is found once, kept in `base`
    /// for the other kinds, and the source index at it is loaded once, kept in
    /// `kept` for the next judgement at the same merge base.
    pub(super) fn raise(
        plan: &Workplan<'_>,
        runner: &dyn Runner,
        prefix: &Prefix,
        change: &str,
        base: &mut Option<MergeBase>,
        kept: &mut Option<BaseIndex>,
    ) -> Result<Vec<Obligation>, LifecycleError> {
        let record = plan.change(change)?;
        let source = plan.source();
        let packages = bound_packages(plan);
        if packages.is_empty() {
            return Ok(Vec::new());
        }
        let now = Surface::of(source, &packages, Side::WorkingTree)?;
        let base = merge_base(base, runner)?;
        let roots = package_roots(source, &packages);
        let current = kept
            .as_ref()
            .is_some_and(|index| index.commit == base.as_str() && index.roots == roots);
        if !current {
            let workspace = load_at(runner, base, prefix, &roots)?;
            *kept = Some(BaseIndex {
                commit: base.as_str().to_string(),
                roots,
                workspace,
            });
        }
        let then_source = kept.as_ref().and_then(|index| index.workspace.as_ref());
        let then = match then_source {
            Some(workspace) => Surface::of(workspace, &packages, Side::MergeBase)?,
            None => Surface::default(),
        };
        let touched = compare(plan, &now, then_source, &then);
        Ok(touched
            .into_iter()
            .map(|touched| Obligation {
                kind: ObligationKind::ContractEffectUnderstated,
                subject: Subject::Change(record.value().header.id.clone()),
                section: Part::Document,
                location: Place {
                    path: record.path().to_string(),
                    line: None,
                },
                evidence: Needed::StatedEffect(touched),
            })
            .collect())
    }
    
    /// The packages that hold an item a `code` binding resolves to.
    fn bound_packages<'p>(plan: &'p Workplan<'_>) -> BTreeSet<&'p str> {
        let source = plan.source();
        plan.graph()
            .bindings()
            .iter()
            .filter(|binding| binding.kind() == BindingKind::Code)
            .flat_map(|binding| binding.items().iter())
            .filter_map(|id| source.item(*id).and_then(|item| source.target(item.target)))
            .map(|target| target.package.as_str())
            .collect()
    }
    
    /// The directories of `packages` in the working tree, sorted: the roots the
    /// index at the merge base is loaded from.
    fn package_roots(source: &Workspace, packages: &BTreeSet<&str>) -> Vec<String> {
        let mut roots: Vec<String> = source
            .packages()
            .iter()
            .filter(|package| packages.contains(package.name.as_str()))
            .map(|package| match package.dir.as_str() {
                "" => ".".to_string(),
                dir => dir.to_string(),
            })
            .collect();
        roots.sort();
        roots.dedup();
        roots
    }
    
    /// The source index of the merge base below `roots`, or `None` when none of
    /// them is a directory there.
    fn load_at(
        runner: &dyn Runner,
        base: &MergeBase,
        prefix: &Prefix,
        roots: &[String],
    ) -> Result<Option<Workspace>, LifecycleError> {
        let tree = BaseTree::new(runner, base, prefix, git::tree_at(runner, base, prefix)?);
        let present: Vec<&str> = roots
            .iter()
            .map(String::as_str)
            .filter(|root| tree.is_dir(root))
            .collect();
        if present.is_empty() {
            return Ok(None);
        }
        match Workspace::load(&tree, &present) {
            Ok(workspace) => Ok(Some(workspace)),
            Err(error) => {
                Err(tree
                    .failure
                    .borrow_mut()
                    .take()
                    .unwrap_or_else(|| LifecycleError::Document {
                        path: error.file.to_string(),
                        side: Side::MergeBase,
                        reason: error.to_string(),
                    }))
            }
        }
    }
    
    /// The identity of a unit on one side: its path, its kind and, for a member
    /// of an impl block, the rendered tokens of the impl's header.
    #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]
    struct Key {
        path: String,
        kind: SymbolKind,
        header: String,
    }
    
    /// A unit of a side: its item and the rendered token stream of its span.
    #[derive(Debug)]
    struct Unit<'w> {
        item: &'w raf_source::Item,
        stream: String,
    }
    
    /// The public surface of one side, as the rule compares it.
    #[derive(Debug, Default)]
    struct Surface<'w> {
        units: BTreeMap<Key, Unit<'w>>,
        /// Each path of the surface, with the unit it names.
        exports: BTreeMap<String, Key>,
    }
    
    impl<'w> Surface<'w> {
        /// The units of the library and binary targets of `packages` in
        /// `source`.
        fn of(
            source: &'w Workspace,
            packages: &BTreeSet<&str>,
            side: Side,
        ) -> Result<Self, LifecycleError> {
            let mut surface = Self::default();
            let mut headers = Headers::default();
            for id in source.target_ids() {
                let Some(target) = source.target(id) else {
                    continue;
                };
                if !matches!(target.kind, TargetKind::Lib | TargetKind::Bin)
                    || !packages.contains(target.package.as_str())
                {
                    continue;
                }
                let exports = source
                    .exported_items(id)
                    .map_err(|error| LifecycleError::Surface {
                        package: target.package.clone(),
                        target: target.crate_path.clone(),
                        side,
                        reason: error.to_string(),
                    })?;
                let mut types = BTreeSet::new();
                for export in &exports.items {
                    let Some(item) = source.item(export.item) else {
                        continue;
                    };
                    let key = surface.add(source, item, &mut headers, side)?;
                    surface.exports.insert(export.path.clone(), key);
                    if matches!(
                        item.kind,
                        SymbolKind::Struct | SymbolKind::Enum | SymbolKind::Union
                    ) {
                        types.insert(item.path.as_str());
                    }
                }
                // The members of the trait impls of an exported type: the
                // surface leaves them out, and they are what the type does.
                let members: Vec<&raf_source::Item> = source
                    .items()
                    .filter(|item| {
                        item.target == id
                            && item.test == TestRole::None
                            && item.trait_path.is_some()
                            && item.self_key == Some(SelfType::Local)
                            && item
                                .path
                                .rsplit_once("::")
                                .is_some_and(|(owner, _)| types.contains(owner))
                    })
                    .collect();
                for item in members {
                    surface.add(source, item, &mut headers, side)?;
                }
            }
            Ok(surface)
        }
    
        /// Add `item` as a unit, once, and return its key.
        fn add(
            &mut self,
            source: &'w Workspace,
            item: &'w raf_source::Item,
            headers: &mut Headers,
            side: Side,
        ) -> Result<Key, LifecycleError> {
            let document = |reason: String| LifecycleError::Document {
                path: item.file.to_string(),
                side,
                reason,
            };
            let text = source
                .source(&item.file)
                .ok_or_else(|| document("is not in the source index".to_string()))?;
            let header = if item.self_type.is_some() {
                headers
                    .of(&item.file, text)
                    .map_err(|error| document(error.to_string()))?
                    .iter()
                    .filter(|impl_block| impl_block.body.contains(&item.span.start_byte))
                    .min_by_key(|impl_block| impl_block.body.len())
                    .map_or_else(
                        || "\u{0}no impl block".to_string(),
                        |impl_block| impl_block.header.clone(),
                    )
            } else {
                String::new()
            };
            let key = Key {
                path: item.path.to_string(),
                kind: item.kind,
                header,
            };
            self.units.entry(key.clone()).or_insert_with(|| Unit {
                item,
                stream: Reader::new(text).stream_of(item.span.start_byte..item.span.end_byte),
            });
            Ok(key)
        }
    }
    
    /// The touched units of `now` against `then`, the surface of `then_source`
    /// at the merge base, sorted by file, line and path.
    fn compare(
        plan: &Workplan<'_>,
        now: &Surface<'_>,
        then_source: Option<&Workspace>,
        then: &Surface<'_>,
    ) -> Vec<Touched> {
        let graph = plan.graph();
        let source = plan.source();
        let at_base = |item: &raf_source::Item| -> Option<&Binding> {
            let same = then_source.map_or(&[][..], |workspace| workspace.same_source(item));
            std::iter::once(item.path.as_str())
                .chain(
                    same.iter()
                        .filter_map(|id| then_source.and_then(|w| w.item(*id)))
                        .map(|item| item.path.as_str()),
                )
                .find_map(|path| covering_path(graph, source, path))
        };
        let mut found: BTreeMap<String, Touched> = BTreeMap::new();
        let mut report = |path: &str, item: &raf_source::Item, binding: &Binding, how: Touch| {
            found.entry(path.to_string()).or_insert_with(|| Touched {
                item: path.to_string(),
                file: item.file.to_string(),
                line: item.name_line,
                capability: binding.capability().to_string(),
                how,
            });
        };
    
        for (key, unit) in &now.units {
            let how = match then.units.get(key) {
                Some(old) if old.stream == unit.stream => continue,
                Some(_) => Touch::Tokens,
                None => Touch::New,
            };
            if let Some(binding) = covering(graph, source, unit.item.id) {
                report(&key.path, unit.item, binding, how);
            }
        }
        for (key, unit) in &then.units {
            if now.units.contains_key(key) {
                continue;
            }
            let Some(binding) = at_base(unit.item) else {
                continue;
            };
            let kept = source
                .items_under(&key.path)
                .find(|item| *item.path == *key.path && item.kind == key.kind);
            match kept {
                Some(item) => report(&key.path, item, binding, Touch::Hidden),
                None => report(&key.path, unit.item, binding, Touch::Removed),
            }
        }
        for (path, key) in &now.exports {
            if *path == key.path {
                continue;
            }
            let how = match then.exports.get(path) {
                Some(old) if old == key => continue,
                Some(_) => Touch::Retargeted,
                None => Touch::New,
            };
            if let Some(unit) = now.units.get(key)
                && let Some(binding) = covering(graph, source, unit.item.id)
            {
                report(path, unit.item, binding, how);
            }
        }
        for (path, key) in &then.exports {
            if *path == key.path || now.exports.contains_key(path) {
                continue;
            }
            if let Some(unit) = then.units.get(key)
                && let Some(binding) = at_base(unit.item)
            {
                report(path, unit.item, binding, Touch::Removed);
            }
        }
    
        let mut touched: Vec<Touched> = found.into_values().collect();
        touched.sort_by(|a, b| (&a.file, a.line, &a.item).cmp(&(&b.file, b.line, &b.item)));
        touched
    }
    
    // --- the headers of impl blocks ----------------------------------------------
    
    /// An impl block of a file: the bytes of its braced body and the rendered
    /// tokens of everything before the brace.
    #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    struct ImplHeader {
        body: Range<usize>,
        header: String,
    }
    
    /// The impl blocks of each file read so far.
    #[derive(Default)]
    struct Headers {
        files: BTreeMap<RelPath, Vec<ImplHeader>>,
    }
    
    impl Headers {
        fn of(&mut self, path: &RelPath, text: &str) -> Result<&[ImplHeader], LoadError> {
            if !self.files.contains_key(path) {
                let read = impl_headers(path, text)?;
                self.files.insert(path.clone(), read);
            }
            Ok(self.files.get(path).map_or(&[][..], Vec::as_slice))
        }
    }
    
    /// Parse `text` through [`parse_source`] and read every impl block of it, at
    /// every depth of inline modules.
    ///
    /// The spans are read on the thread that parsed, which holds the location
    /// data of the parse, and leave it as plain values.
    fn impl_headers(path: &RelPath, text: &str) -> Result<Vec<ImplHeader>, LoadError> {
        parse_source(path, text, |file| {
            let reader = Reader::new(text);
            let mut out = Vec::new();
            reader.impls(&file.items, &mut out);
            out
        })
    }
    
    /// The text of a file with the byte offset where each line starts.
    struct Reader<'t> {
        text: &'t str,
        starts: Vec<usize>,
    }
    
    impl<'t> Reader<'t> {
        fn new(text: &'t str) -> Self {
            let starts = std::iter::once(0)
                .chain(text.match_indices('\n').map(|(at, _)| at + 1))
                .collect();
            Self { text, starts }
        }
    
        /// The byte offset of a line and column of the parse: the line is 1-based
        /// and the column counts characters from 0.
        fn byte(&self, at: LineColumn) -> Option<usize> {
            let start = *self.starts.get(at.line.checked_sub(1)?)?;
            let line = self.text.get(start..)?;
            let line = line.split('\n').next().unwrap_or(line);
            let offset = line
                .char_indices()
                .map(|(offset, _)| offset)
                .chain(std::iter::once(line.len()))
                .nth(at.column)?;
            Some(start + offset)
        }
    
        /// The rendered stream of the bytes `range`.
        fn stream_of(&self, range: Range<usize>) -> String {
            let Some(slice) = self.text.get(range.clone()) else {
                // A span that is not a range of the text names no item of it.
                return format!("\u{0}no text at {}..{}", range.start, range.end);
            };
            let slice = slice.replace("\r\n", "\n");
            match slice.parse::<TokenStream>() {
                Ok(stream) => {
                    let mut out = String::new();
                    render(stream, &mut out);
                    out
                }
                // The parse accepted the file, so its items lex; a slice that does
                // not is compared by its text, which no stream equals.
                Err(_) => format!("\u{0}{slice}"),
            }
        }
    
        /// Read the impl blocks of `items` and of the inline modules among them.
        fn impls(&self, items: &[Item], out: &mut Vec<ImplHeader>) {
            for item in items {
                match item {
                    Item::Impl(block) => {
                        let start = self.byte(block.span().start());
                        let open = self.byte(block.brace_token.span.open().start());
                        let close = self.byte(block.brace_token.span.close().end());
                        if let (Some(start), Some(open), Some(close)) = (start, open, close)
                            && start <= open
                            && open <= close
                        {
                            out.push(ImplHeader {
                                body: open..close,
                                header: self.stream_of(start..open),
                            });
                        }
                    }
                    Item::Mod(module) => {
                        if let Some((_, content)) = &module.content {
                            self.impls(content, out);
                        }
                    }
                    _ => {}
                }
            }
        }
    }
    
    /// Write `stream` as a comparison key: each identifier and literal as its
    /// text between spaces, each punctuation character followed by a space unless
    /// it is joined to the next, and each group between its delimiters. Spans,
    /// whitespace and comments that are not doc comments leave no trace.
    fn render(stream: TokenStream, out: &mut String) {
        for tree in stream {
            match tree {
                TokenTree::Group(group) => {
                    let (open, close) = match group.delimiter() {
                        Delimiter::Parenthesis => ('(', ')'),
                        Delimiter::Brace => ('{', '}'),
                        Delimiter::Bracket => ('[', ']'),
                        Delimiter::None => ('\u{2}', '\u{3}'),
                    };
                    out.push(open);
                    render(group.stream(), out);
                    out.push(close);
                }
                TokenTree::Ident(ident) => {
                    out.push(' ');
                    out.push_str(&ident.to_string());
                    out.push(' ');
                }
                TokenTree::Punct(punct) => {
                    out.push(punct.as_char());
                    if punct.spacing() == Spacing::Alone {
                        out.push(' ');
                    }
                }
                TokenTree::Literal(literal) => {
                    out.push(' ');
                    out.push_str(&literal.to_string());
                    out.push(' ');
                }
            }
        }
    }
    
    // --- the files of the merge base ---------------------------------------------
    
    /// The most symbolic links one read follows before it is a loop.
    const MAX_LINKS: usize = 40;
    
    /// The files of the merge base below the workspace root, as a [`Tree`]: the
    /// listing answers every question of entries and directories, and a file is
    /// read with the blob row the first time the load reads it.
    struct BaseTree<'r> {
        runner: &'r dyn Runner,
        base: &'r MergeBase,
        prefix: &'r Prefix,
        files: BTreeMap<String, TreeEntry>,
        dirs: BTreeSet<String>,
        texts: RefCell<BTreeMap<String, String>>,
        /// The first failure of `git`, which the load can only report as an
        /// error of the tree.
        failure: RefCell<Option<LifecycleError>>,
    }
    
    impl<'r> BaseTree<'r> {
        fn new(
            runner: &'r dyn Runner,
            base: &'r MergeBase,
            prefix: &'r Prefix,
            listing: Vec<(RelPath, TreeEntry)>,
        ) -> Self {
            let mut files = BTreeMap::new();
            let mut dirs = BTreeSet::from([String::new()]);
            for (path, kind) in listing {
                let key = path.as_str().to_string();
                let mut at = key.as_str();
                while let Some((parent, _)) = at.rsplit_once('/') {
                    dirs.insert(parent.to_string());
                    at = parent;
                }
                files.insert(key, kind);
            }
            Self {
                runner,
                base,
                prefix,
                files,
                dirs,
                texts: RefCell::new(BTreeMap::new()),
                failure: RefCell::new(None),
            }
        }
    
        fn is_dir(&self, root: &str) -> bool {
            let key = if root == "." { "" } else { root };
            self.dirs.contains(key)
        }
    
        /// The key of the file `path` names, after its symbolic links.
        fn file_key(&self, path: &RelPath) -> Result<String, TreeError> {
            let mut key = collapse(path.as_str()).ok_or_else(|| TreeError::OutsideRoot {
                path: path.to_string(),
            })?;
            for _ in 0..MAX_LINKS {
                match self.files.get(&key) {
                    Some(TreeEntry::File) => return Ok(key),
                    Some(TreeEntry::Symlink) => {
                        let target = self.text(&key, path)?;
                        let joined = match key.rsplit_once('/') {
                            _ if target.starts_with('/') => None,
                            Some((dir, _)) => collapse(&format!("{dir}/{target}")),
                            None => collapse(&target),
                        };
                        key = joined.ok_or_else(|| TreeError::OutsideRoot {
                            path: path.to_string(),
                        })?;
                    }
                    Some(TreeEntry::Submodule) => {
                        return Err(TreeError::NotRegular {
                            path: path.to_string(),
                        });
                    }
                    None if self.dirs.contains(&key) => {
                        return Err(TreeError::NotRegular {
                            path: path.to_string(),
                        });
                    }
                    None => {
                        return Err(TreeError::NotFound {
                            path: path.to_string(),
                        });
                    }
                }
            }
            Err(TreeError::SymlinkLoop {
                path: path.to_string(),
            })
        }
    
        /// The text of the listed file `key`, read once.
        fn text(&self, key: &str, path: &RelPath) -> Result<String, TreeError> {
            if let Some(text) = self.texts.borrow().get(key) {
                return Ok(text.clone());
            }
            let bytes = match blob(self.runner, self.base, self.prefix, key) {
                Ok(Some(bytes)) => bytes,
                Ok(None) => {
                    return Err(TreeError::NotFound {
                        path: path.to_string(),
                    });
                }
                Err(error) => {
                    let reason = error.to_string();
                    self.failure.borrow_mut().get_or_insert(error);
                    return Err(TreeError::Io {
                        path: path.to_string(),
                        source: io::Error::other(reason),
                    });
                }
            };
            let text = String::from_utf8(bytes).map_err(|_| TreeError::NotUtf8 {
                path: path.to_string(),
            })?;
            self.texts
                .borrow_mut()
                .insert(key.to_string(), text.clone());
            Ok(text)
        }
    }
    
    impl Tree for BaseTree<'_> {
        fn read(&self, path: &RelPath, max_bytes: u64) -> Result<String, TreeError> {
            let key = self.file_key(path)?;
            let text = self.text(&key, path)?;
            if u64::try_from(text.len()).map_or(true, |len| len > max_bytes) {
                return Err(TreeError::TooLarge {
                    path: path.to_string(),
                    limit: max_bytes,
                });
            }
            Ok(text)
        }
    
        fn read_unseen(
            &self,
            path: &RelPath,
            max_bytes: u64,
            seen: &dyn Fn(&FileId) -> bool,
        ) -> Result<TreeRead, TreeError> {
            let key = self.file_key(path)?;
            let id = FileId::from_key(&key);
            if seen(&id) {
                return Ok(TreeRead::Seen(id));
            }
            Ok(TreeRead::Read {
                id,
                text: self.read(path, max_bytes)?,
            })
        }
    
        fn list(&self, path: &RelPath) -> Result<Vec<Entry>, TreeError> {
            let key = collapse(path.as_str()).ok_or_else(|| TreeError::OutsideRoot {
                path: path.to_string(),
            })?;
            if self.files.contains_key(&key) {
                return Err(TreeError::NotRegular {
                    path: path.to_string(),
                });
            }
            if !self.dirs.contains(&key) {
                return Err(TreeError::NotFound {
                    path: path.to_string(),
                });
            }
            let below = if key.is_empty() {
                String::new()
            } else {
                format!("{key}/")
            };
            let mut entries: BTreeMap<&str, EntryKind> = BTreeMap::new();
            for (file, kind) in self.files.range(below.clone()..) {
                let Some(rest) = file.strip_prefix(&below) else {
                    break;
                };
                match rest.split_once('/') {
                    Some((dir, _)) => {
                        entries.insert(dir, EntryKind::Dir);
                    }
                    None => {
                        entries.insert(
                            rest,
                            match kind {
                                TreeEntry::File => EntryKind::File,
                                TreeEntry::Symlink => EntryKind::Symlink,
                                TreeEntry::Submodule => EntryKind::Other,
                            },
                        );
                    }
                }
            }
            Ok(entries
                .into_iter()
                .map(|(name, kind)| Entry::new(name, kind))
                .collect())
        }
    
        fn entry(&self, path: &RelPath) -> Result<Option<EntryKind>, TreeError> {
            let Some(key) = collapse(path.as_str()) else {
                return Err(TreeError::OutsideRoot {
                    path: path.to_string(),
                });
            };
            Ok(match self.files.get(&key) {
                Some(TreeEntry::File) => Some(EntryKind::File),
                Some(TreeEntry::Symlink) => Some(EntryKind::Symlink),
                Some(TreeEntry::Submodule) => Some(EntryKind::Other),
                None if self.dirs.contains(&key) => Some(EntryKind::Dir),
                None => None,
            })
        }
    }
    
    /// The lexical form of a relative path, `.` dropped and `..` applied against
    /// its own segments; `None` when it climbs above the root.
    fn collapse(path: &str) -> Option<String> {
        let mut kept: Vec<&str> = Vec::new();
        for segment in path.split('/') {
            match segment {
                "" | "." => {}
                ".." => {
                    kept.pop()?;
                }
                other => kept.push(other),
            }
        }
        Some(kept.join("/"))
    }
