    mod tests;
  in crates/raf-workplan/src/lifecycle/touched/tests.rs
    // SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2026 Blackcat Informatics® Inc. <paudley@blackcatinformatics.ca>
    // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT OR Apache-2.0 OR MulanPSL-2.0
    
    //! The units of a side, the token streams they are compared by, the headers
    //! of impl blocks and the tree of the merge base, against memory trees and
    //! scripted `git` answers.
    
    use std::collections::BTreeSet;
    
    use raf_source::{EntryKind, MemoryTree, RelPath, Tree, TreeError, Workspace};
    
    use super::{BaseTree, Reader, Surface, impl_headers, load_at};
    use crate::exec::{Call, Commit, FakeRunner, Output, Program};
    use crate::git::{self, GitError, MergeBase, Prefix};
    use crate::lifecycle::{LifecycleError, Side};
    
    const MANIFEST: &str = "[package]\nname = \"demo\"\nversion = \"0.1.0\"\nedition = \"2021\"\n";
    const COMMIT: &str = "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef01234567";
    
    fn path(text: &str) -> RelPath {
        RelPath::new(text).expect("a path")
    }
    
    /// The workspace of one library whose `src/lib.rs` is `lib`.
    fn workspace(lib: &str) -> Workspace {
        Workspace::load(
            &MemoryTree::from([("Cargo.toml", MANIFEST), ("src/lib.rs", lib)]),
            &["."],
        )
        .expect("the library loads")
    }
    
    /// The rendered stream of the item `item` of the library `lib`, read as the
    /// rule reads it: the bytes of its span in the source index.
    fn stream(lib: &str, item: &str) -> String {
        let source = workspace(lib);
        let found = source
            .items()
            .find(|candidate| *candidate.path == *item)
            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("no {item}"));
        let text = source.source(&found.file).expect("indexed");
        Reader::new(text).stream_of(found.span.start_byte..found.span.end_byte)
    }
    
    /// The rendered stream of `text` as a whole.
    fn rendered(text: &str) -> String {
        Reader::new(text).stream_of(0..text.len())
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn whitespace_and_plain_comments_leave_the_stream_and_a_doc_comment_or_a_token_changes_it() {
        let base = "/// Adds.\npub fn add(a: u32, b: u32) -> u32 {\n    a + b\n}\n";
        let same = [
            "/// Adds.\npub fn add(a: u32, b: u32) -> u32 { a + b }\n",
            "/// Adds.\npub fn add(\n    a: u32,\n    b: u32\n) -> u32 {\n    // plain\n    a + b /* block */\n}\n",
            "/// Adds.\r\npub fn add(a: u32, b: u32) -> u32 {\r\n    a + b\r\n}\r\n",
            "/// Adds.\npub fn add(a:u32,b:u32)->u32{\n\ta+b\n}\n",
            "#[doc = \" Adds.\"]\npub fn add(a: u32, b: u32) -> u32 {\n    a + b\n}\n",
        ];
        for text in same {
            assert_eq!(
                stream(text, "demo::add"),
                stream(base, "demo::add"),
                "{text}"
            );
        }
        // A trailing comma is a token, so a reflow that adds one (as rustfmt does
        // for one parameter per line) touches the item; so does a trailing space
        // inside a doc comment, which is text of its `#[doc]` attribute.
        let different = [
            "/// Adds two.\npub fn add(a: u32, b: u32) -> u32 {\n    a + b\n}\n",
            "/// Adds. \npub fn add(a: u32, b: u32) -> u32 {\n    a + b\n}\n",
            "/** Adds. */\npub fn add(a: u32, b: u32) -> u32 {\n    a + b\n}\n",
            "/// Adds.\npub fn add(a: u32, b: u32) -> u32 {\n    b + a\n}\n",
            "/// Adds.\n#[must_use]\npub fn add(a: u32, b: u32) -> u32 {\n    a + b\n}\n",
            "/// Adds.\npub(crate) fn add(a: u32, b: u32) -> u32 {\n    a + b\n}\n",
            "/// Adds.\npub fn add(a: u64, b: u32) -> u32 {\n    a + b\n}\n",
            "/// Adds.\npub fn add(\n    a: u32,\n    b: u32,\n) -> u32 {\n    a + b\n}\n",
        ];
        for text in different {
            assert_ne!(
                stream(text, "demo::add"),
                stream(base, "demo::add"),
                "{text}"
            );
        }
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn the_surface_holds_the_public_items_impl_members_by_their_header_and_trait_impl_members_of_public_types_only()
     {
        let lib = "pub mod inner {\n    pub fn go() {}\n    fn helper() {}\n}\n#[derive(PartialEq)]\npub struct Frame;\nimpl Frame {\n    pub fn push(&self) {}\n    fn grow(&self) {}\n}\nimpl Clone for Frame {\n    fn clone(&self) -> Self { Frame }\n}\nimpl Eq for Frame {}\npub trait Shape {\n    fn area(&self) -> u32;\n}\nstruct Quiet;\nimpl Clone for Quiet {\n    fn clone(&self) -> Self { Quiet }\n}\n#[doc(hidden)]\npub fn hidden() {}\npub trait Foreign { fn foreign(&self); }\nimpl Foreign for Vec<u8> { fn foreign(&self) {} }\npub(crate) fn narrow() {}\nmacro_rules! make { () => {} }\npub use inner::go as run;\n";
        let source = workspace(lib);
        let packages = BTreeSet::from(["demo"]);
        let surface = Surface::of(&source, &packages, Side::WorkingTree).expect("a surface");
        let keys: Vec<(&str, String)> = surface
            .units
            .keys()
            .map(|key| (key.path.as_str(), key.header.clone()))
            .collect();
        assert_eq!(
            keys,
            [
                ("demo::Foreign", String::new()),
                ("demo::Foreign::foreign", String::new()),
                ("demo::Frame", String::new()),
                ("demo::Frame::clone", rendered("impl Clone for Frame")),
                ("demo::Frame::push", rendered("impl Frame")),
                ("demo::Shape", String::new()),
                ("demo::Shape::area", String::new()),
                ("demo::inner", String::new()),
                ("demo::inner::go", String::new()),
            ]
        );
        // Each path of the surface names its unit: a re-export names the item it
        // exports.
        assert_eq!(
            surface
                .exports
                .get("demo::run")
                .map(|key| key.path.as_str()),
            Some("demo::inner::go")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            surface
                .exports
                .get("demo::inner::go")
                .map(|key| key.path.as_str()),
            Some("demo::inner::go")
        );
        // Removing excluded syntax changes no compared identity or token stream:
        // an empty impl, a hidden function and an impl for a foreign self type.
        let reduced = lib
            .replace("impl Eq for Frame {}", "")
            .replace("#[doc(hidden)]\npub fn hidden() {}", "")
            .replace("impl Foreign for Vec<u8> { fn foreign(&self) {} }", "");
        let reduced_source = workspace(&reduced);
        let reduced_surface =
            Surface::of(&reduced_source, &packages, Side::WorkingTree).expect("a reduced surface");
        let entries = |surface: &Surface<'_>| {
            surface
                .units
                .iter()
                .map(|(key, unit)| (key.clone(), unit.stream.clone()))
                .collect::<Vec<_>>()
        };
        assert_eq!(entries(&surface), entries(&reduced_surface));
        assert_eq!(surface.exports, reduced_surface.exports);
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn an_impl_header_is_the_tokens_before_its_brace_at_every_depth_and_a_file_that_does_not_parse_is_an_error()
     {
        let text = "pub struct W<T>(T);\n#[cfg(any())]\nimpl<T: Clone> W<T> where T: Copy {\n    pub fn get(&self) {}\n}\nmod m {\n    impl super::W<u8> {}\n}\n";
        let headers = impl_headers(&path("src/lib.rs"), text).expect("it parses");
        let first = text.find("impl<").expect("the first impl") - "#[cfg(any())]\n".len();
        let open = text.find(" {\n    pub fn").expect("its brace") + 1;
        assert_eq!(
            headers[0].header,
            rendered("#[cfg(any())]\nimpl<T: Clone> W<T> where T: Copy")
        );
        assert_eq!(headers[0].body.start, open);
        assert_eq!(
            &text[first..open],
            "#[cfg(any())]\nimpl<T: Clone> W<T> where T: Copy "
        );
        assert!(
            headers[0]
                .body
                .contains(&text.find("pub fn get").expect("get"))
        );
        assert_eq!(headers[1].header, rendered("impl super::W<u8>"));
        assert_eq!(headers.len(), 2);
        assert!(impl_headers(&path("src/lib.rs"), "pub fn (").is_err());
    }
    
    fn said(text: &str) -> Output {
        Output::new(Some(0), text.as_bytes().to_vec(), Vec::new())
    }
    
    /// The merge base of the scripted answers.
    fn base() -> MergeBase {
        let commit = Commit::new(COMMIT).expect("a commit id");
        let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(
            Program::Git,
            &["merge-base", "HEAD", COMMIT],
            Ok(said(&format!("{COMMIT}\n"))),
        );
        git::merge_base(&runner, &commit).expect("a merge base")
    }
    
    /// The prefix `text` as `git rev-parse --show-prefix` prints it.
    fn prefix(text: &str) -> Prefix {
        let runner = FakeRunner::new().on(
            Program::Git,
            &["rev-parse", "--show-prefix"],
            Ok(said(&format!("{text}\n"))),
        );
        git::show_prefix(&runner).expect("a prefix")
    }
    
    const LISTING: &str = "100644 blob 1111111111111111111111111111111111111111\tCargo.toml\x00100644 blob 2222222222222222222222222222222222222222\tsrc/lib.rs\x00120000 blob 3333333333333333333333333333333333333333\tsrc/link.rs\x00160000 commit 4444444444444444444444444444444444444444\tvendor/sub\x00100644 blob 5555555555555555555555555555555555555555\tother/README.md\x00";
    
    /// A runner that answers the listing below `top` and the blob of each of
    /// `files`, named from the top of the work tree.
    fn scripted(top: &str, files: &[(&str, &str)]) -> FakeRunner {
        let mut listing = String::new();
        for record in LISTING.split('\0').filter(|record| !record.is_empty()) {
            let (head, name) = record.split_once('\t').expect("a record");
            listing.push_str(head);
            listing.push('\t');
            if !name.starts_with("other/") {
                listing.push_str(top);
            }
            listing.push_str(name);
            listing.push('\0');
        }
        let mut runner = FakeRunner::new().on(
            Program::Git,
            &["ls-tree", "-r", "-z", "--full-tree", COMMIT],
            Ok(said(&listing)),
        );
        for (name, text) in files {
            runner = runner.on(
                Program::Git,
                &["cat-file", "blob", &format!("{COMMIT}:{top}{name}")],
                Ok(said(text)),
            );
        }
        runner
    }
    
    fn commands(runner: &FakeRunner) -> Vec<String> {
        runner.calls().iter().map(Call::command).collect()
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn the_tree_of_the_merge_base_answers_from_one_listing_and_reads_each_file_once_with_the_blob_row()
    {
        for top in ["", "services/api/"] {
            let runner = scripted(
                top,
                &[
                    ("Cargo.toml", MANIFEST),
                    ("src/lib.rs", "pub fn go() {}\n"),
                    ("src/link.rs", "lib.rs"),
                ],
            );
            let (base, prefix) = (base(), prefix(top));
            let listing = git::tree_at(&runner, &base, &prefix).expect("a listing");
            let tree = BaseTree::new(&runner, &base, &prefix, listing);
    
            // The listing names the files below the prefix and their directories.
            assert_eq!(
                tree.entry(&path("src")).expect("an answer"),
                Some(EntryKind::Dir)
            );
            assert_eq!(
                tree.entry(&path("src/lib.rs")).expect("an answer"),
                Some(EntryKind::File)
            );
            assert_eq!(
                tree.entry(&path("src/link.rs")).expect("an answer"),
                Some(EntryKind::Symlink)
            );
            assert_eq!(
                tree.entry(&path("vendor/sub")).expect("an answer"),
                Some(EntryKind::Other)
            );
            assert_eq!(
                tree.entry(&path("src/missing.rs")).expect("an answer"),
                None
            );
            assert_eq!(
                tree.entry(&path("other")).expect("an answer"),
                top.is_empty().then_some(EntryKind::Dir),
                "{top}"
            );
            let names: Vec<(String, EntryKind)> = tree
                .list(&RelPath::root())
                .expect("the root")
                .into_iter()
                .map(|entry| (entry.name, entry.kind))
                .collect();
            let mut expected = vec![
                ("Cargo.toml".to_string(), EntryKind::File),
                ("src".to_string(), EntryKind::Dir),
                ("vendor".to_string(), EntryKind::Dir),
            ];
            if top.is_empty() {
                expected.insert(1, ("other".to_string(), EntryKind::Dir));
            }
            assert_eq!(names, expected);
            assert!(matches!(
                tree.list(&path("src/lib.rs")),
                Err(TreeError::NotRegular { .. })
            ));
            assert!(matches!(
                tree.read(&path("src"), 1024),
                Err(TreeError::NotRegular { .. })
            ));
            assert!(matches!(
                tree.read(&path("src/missing.rs"), 1024),
                Err(TreeError::NotFound { .. })
            ));
            assert!(matches!(
                tree.read(&path("src/lib.rs"), 3),
                Err(TreeError::TooLarge { .. })
            ));
    
            // A file is read once however often the load asks, and a link reads
            // the file it names.
            assert_eq!(
                tree.read(&path("src/lib.rs"), 1024).expect("a text"),
                "pub fn go() {}\n"
            );
            assert_eq!(
                tree.read(&path("src/link.rs"), 1024).expect("a text"),
                "pub fn go() {}\n"
            );
            let source = Workspace::load(&tree, &["."]).expect("the merge base loads");
            assert!(source.items().any(|item| *item.path == *"demo::go"));
            let asked = commands(&runner);
            assert_eq!(
                asked,
                [
                    format!("git ls-tree -r -z --full-tree {COMMIT}"),
                    format!("git cat-file blob {COMMIT}:{top}src/lib.rs"),
                    format!("git cat-file blob {COMMIT}:{top}src/link.rs"),
                    format!("git cat-file blob {COMMIT}:{top}Cargo.toml"),
                ],
                "{top}"
            );
        }
    }
    
    #[test]
    fn a_git_failure_while_the_merge_base_loads_is_that_error_and_a_file_that_is_not_utf8_names_the_file()
     {
        // `src/lib.rs` has no scripted answer, so `git` fails on it.
        let runner = scripted("", &[("Cargo.toml", MANIFEST)]);
        let (base, prefix) = (base(), prefix(""));
        let error = load_at(&runner, &base, &prefix, &[".".to_string()]).expect_err("no answer");
        assert!(
            matches!(&error, LifecycleError::Git(GitError::Exec(_))),
            "{error:?}"
        );
    
        let runner = scripted("", &[("Cargo.toml", MANIFEST)]).on(
            Program::Git,
            &["cat-file", "blob", &format!("{COMMIT}:src/lib.rs")],
            Ok(Output::new(Some(0), vec![0xff, 0xfe], Vec::new())),
        );
        let error = load_at(&runner, &base, &prefix, &[".".to_string()]).expect_err("not UTF-8");
        assert!(
            matches!(
                &error,
                LifecycleError::Document { path, side: Side::MergeBase, .. } if path == "src/lib.rs"
            ),
            "{error:?}"
        );
    
        // The neighbour: the same tree with a text file loads, and a root that is
        // not a directory at the merge base loads nothing and reads nothing.
        let runner = scripted(
            "",
            &[("Cargo.toml", MANIFEST), ("src/lib.rs", "pub fn go() {}\n")],
        );
        assert!(
            load_at(&runner, &base, &prefix, &[".".to_string()])
                .expect("it loads")
                .is_some()
        );
        let runner = scripted("", &[]);
        assert!(
            load_at(&runner, &base, &prefix, &["crates/gone".to_string()])
                .expect("nothing to load")
                .is_none()
        );
        assert_eq!(
            commands(&runner),
            [format!("git ls-tree -r -z --full-tree {COMMIT}")]
        );
    }
  in crates/raf-workplan/src/lifecycle/untouched.rs
    // SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2026 Blackcat Informatics® Inc. <paudley@blackcatinformatics.ca>
    // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT OR Apache-2.0 OR MulanPSL-2.0
    
    //! `section-untouched`: a section the change affects whose authored text is
    //! the text it had at the merge base.
    //!
    //! "Authored text" is what a person writes. The text of a generated block
    //! (`<!-- raf:generated:... -->` up to its end marker) is written by
    //! `docs sync` and moves whenever a binding or a relation moves, so it is left
    //! out of the comparison on both sides; the marker lines, fences, PROPOSED
    //! blocks and every other line stay. A section whose only change is a
    //! regenerated block is therefore still owed.
    
    use std::collections::BTreeMap;
    use std::fmt;
    use std::ops::Range;
    
    use raf_okf::{Body, CapabilitySection, ContractEffect, MAX_TEXT_BYTES};
    use raf_source::{RelPath, Tree};
    
    use crate::SectionId;
    use crate::exec::{Runner, SourcePath};
    use crate::git::{self, MergeBase, Prefix};
    use crate::lookup::capability_path;
    use crate::obligation::{Needed, Obligation, ObligationKind, Part, Place, Subject, suppressed};
    use crate::{Opened, lifecycle::Impact};
    
    use super::session::{LifecycleError, Side};
    
    /// The authored text of one section of a body: its heading and its content
    /// with the content of every generated block taken out, in LF line endings.
    ///
    /// Two digests are equal exactly when a person wrote the same text. The value
    /// holds the text itself and not a hash, so equal digests never collide; its
    /// `Debug` shows the length only.
    #[derive(Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    pub struct SectionDigest(String);
    
    impl SectionDigest {
        /// The authored text the digest stands for.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn text(&self) -> &str {
            &self.0
        }
    }
    
    impl fmt::Debug for SectionDigest {
        fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
            write!(f, "SectionDigest({} bytes)", self.0.len())
        }
    }
    
    /// The digest of the section `section` of `body`, or `None` when the body has
    /// no such section.
    ///
    /// The text is the heading and the content of the section. The content of
    /// each generated block inside it is taken out, between its two marker lines
    /// and not the markers, so a regenerated block does not change the digest and
    /// an added or removed marker does. CRLF line endings count as LF, so a
    /// checkout that converts line endings does not touch every section.
    ///
    /// Every other difference is a difference of authored text: a whitespace-only
    /// edit (trailing spaces, a blank line, a final newline) and a section moved to
    /// another place in the file both make the digest differ, so either clears a
    /// `section-untouched` obligation. The body is read with [`Body::parse`] and is
    /// not validated against the profile, so a heading that appears twice (a
    /// document at the merge base that the loader would refuse) is digested from its
    /// first occurrence.
    ///
    /// ```rust
    /// use raf_okf::{Body, CapabilitySection};
    /// use raf_workplan::lifecycle::section_digest;
    ///
    /// let before = Body::parse(
    ///     "# T\n\n## Contract\n\nIt reads.\n<!-- raf:generated:matrix -->\nold row\n<!-- raf:generated:end -->\n",
    /// )?;
    /// let synced = Body::parse(
    ///     "# T\n\n## Contract\n\nIt reads.\n<!-- raf:generated:matrix -->\nnew row\n<!-- raf:generated:end -->\n",
    /// )?;
    /// let edited = Body::parse(
    ///     "# T\n\n## Contract\n\nIt reads and writes.\n<!-- raf:generated:matrix -->\nold row\n<!-- raf:generated:end -->\n",
    /// )?;
    /// let digest = |body: &Body| section_digest(body, CapabilitySection::Contract);
    /// assert_eq!(digest(&before), digest(&synced));
    /// assert_ne!(digest(&before), digest(&edited));
    /// assert_eq!(section_digest(&before, CapabilitySection::Usage), None);
    /// # Ok::<(), raf_okf::ParseError>(())
    /// ```
    #[must_use]
    pub fn section_digest(body: &Body, section: CapabilitySection) -> Option<SectionDigest> {
        let found = body
            .sections()
            .iter()
            .find(|candidate| candidate.slug() == section.slug())?;
        let text = body.section_text(found)?;
        let base = body.span().bytes.start;
        let start = found.content().bytes.start.checked_sub(base)?;
        let end = found.content().bytes.end.checked_sub(base)?;
    
        let mut blanked: Vec<Range<usize>> = body
            .generated_blocks()
            .iter()
            .filter_map(|block| {
                let bytes = &block.content().bytes;
                let from = bytes.start.checked_sub(base)?.max(start);
                let to = bytes.end.checked_sub(base)?.min(end);
                (from < to).then_some(from..to)
            })
            .collect();
        blanked.sort_by_key(|range| range.start);
    
        let mut authored = String::with_capacity(text.len());
        authored.push_str(found.heading());
        authored.push('\n');
        let mut at = start;
        for range in blanked {
            if range.start >= at {
                authored.push_str(body.text().get(at..range.start)?);
                at = range.end;
            }
        }
        authored.push_str(body.text().get(at..end)?);
        Some(SectionDigest(authored.replace("\r\n", "\n")))
    }
    
    /// The `section-untouched` obligations of a change: the affected sections
    /// whose authored text is the text at the merge base.
    ///
    /// Nothing is asked of `git` when every section of `affects` is left out by
    /// the effect of the change ([`suppressed`]). Otherwise the merge base is found once
    /// ([`git::default_merge_base`], kept in `base` for the other process-backed
    /// kinds) and each capability file is read once at it.
    pub(super) fn raise(
        opened: &Opened,
        runner: &dyn Runner,
        prefix: &Prefix,
        impact: &Impact,
        effect: ContractEffect,
        base: &mut Option<MergeBase>,
    ) -> Result<Vec<Obligation>, LifecycleError> {
        let wanted: Vec<&SectionId> = impact
            .sections
            .iter()
            .filter(|id| {
                !suppressed(
                    ObligationKind::SectionUntouched,
                    Part::Section(id.section()),
                    effect,
                )
            })
            .collect();
        if wanted.is_empty() {
            return Ok(Vec::new());
        }
        let base = merge_base(base, runner)?;
        let mut files: BTreeMap<String, Files> = BTreeMap::new();
        let mut owed = Vec::new();
        for id in wanted {
            let path = capability_path(id.capability()).ok_or_else(|| LifecycleError::Document {
                path: id.capability().to_string(),
                side: Side::WorkingTree,
                reason: "is not a capability id".to_string(),
            })?;
            if !files.contains_key(&path) {
                let read = Files::read(opened, runner, base, prefix, &path)?;
                files.insert(path.clone(), read);
            }
            let Some(file) = files.get(&path) else {
                continue;
            };
            if file.untouched(id.section(), &path)? {
                owed.push(Obligation {
                    kind: ObligationKind::SectionUntouched,
                    subject: Subject::Capability(id.capability().to_string()),
                    section: Part::Section(id.section()),
                    location: Place { path, line: None },
                    evidence: Needed::SectionDiff,
                });
            }
        }
        Ok(owed)
    }
    
    /// The merge base of `HEAD` and the default branch, asked of `git` the first
    /// time a kind needs it and kept in `slot` for the next.
    pub(super) fn merge_base<'b>(
        slot: &'b mut Option<MergeBase>,
        runner: &dyn Runner,
    ) -> Result<&'b MergeBase, LifecycleError> {
        let base = match slot.take() {
            Some(base) => base,
            None => git::default_merge_base(runner)?,
        };
        Ok(slot.insert(base))
    }
    
    /// The file `path` (repository-relative, as the loaded documents name it) at
    /// the merge base, named from the top of the work tree, or `None` when it is
    /// not there.
    pub(super) fn blob(
        runner: &dyn Runner,
        base: &MergeBase,
        prefix: &Prefix,
        path: &str,
    ) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, LifecycleError> {
        let document = |reason: String| LifecycleError::Document {
            path: path.to_string(),
            side: Side::WorkingTree,
            reason,
        };
        let source = SourcePath::new(path)
            .map_err(|error| document(format!("is not a repository path: {error}")))?;
        // `git` names the file from the top of the work tree.
        let named = prefix.join(&source).map_err(|error| {
            document(format!(
                "cannot be named below `{}`: {error}",
                prefix.as_str()
            ))
        })?;
        Ok(git::blob_at(runner, base, &named)?)
    }
    
    /// One capability file at the merge base and in the working tree.
    struct Files {
        /// The body at the merge base, or `None` when the file is not there.
        base: Option<Body>,
        now: Body,
    }
    
    impl Files {
        fn read(
            opened: &Opened,
            runner: &dyn Runner,
            base: &MergeBase,
            prefix: &Prefix,
            path: &str,
        ) -> Result<Self, LifecycleError> {
            let document = |side, reason: String| LifecycleError::Document {
                path: path.to_string(),
                side,
                reason,
            };
            let base = blob(runner, base, prefix, path)?
                .map(|bytes| {
                    let text = String::from_utf8(bytes)
                        .map_err(|_| document(Side::MergeBase, "is not UTF-8 text".to_string()))?;
                    Body::parse(&text).map_err(|error| document(Side::MergeBase, error.to_string()))
                })
                .transpose()?;
            let relative = RelPath::new(path)
                .map_err(|error| document(Side::WorkingTree, format!("is not a tree path: {error}")))?;
            let text = opened
                .tree()
                .read(&relative, MAX_TEXT_BYTES as u64)
                .map_err(|error| document(Side::WorkingTree, error.to_string()))?;
            let now =
                Body::parse(&text).map_err(|error| document(Side::WorkingTree, error.to_string()))?;
            Ok(Self { base, now })
        }
    
        /// Whether the section has the authored text it had at the merge base. A
        /// file or a section that is not there counts as touched.
        fn untouched(&self, section: CapabilitySection, path: &str) -> Result<bool, LifecycleError> {
            let Some(now) = section_digest(&self.now, section) else {
                return Err(LifecycleError::Document {
                    path: path.to_string(),
                    side: Side::WorkingTree,
                    reason: format!("has no `{}` section", section.slug()),
                });
            };
            let Some(base) = &self.base else {
                return Ok(false);
            };
            Ok(section_digest(base, section).is_some_and(|then| then == now))
        }
    }

    // SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2026 Blackcat Informatics® Inc. <paudley@blackcatinformatics.ca>

    //! `verify`: run the bound tests and examples and report each binding.
    
    use std::collections::BTreeSet;
    use std::fmt;
    
    use crate::bound::{BoundOutcome, Evidence, Executor};
    use crate::exec::Runner;
    use crate::lookup::capability_path;
    use crate::obligation::ReconcileError;
    use crate::{Binding, BindingKind, Opened, Workplan};
    
    use super::session::LifecycleError;
    
    /// The most characters of a reason one line of a report shows, not counting
    /// the gap in the middle of a longer one.
    const REASON: usize = 240;
    
    /// What a `verify` run found: the [`Evidence`] of each binding it ran, in
    /// order of capability, then tests before examples, then path.
    ///
    /// `Display` is one line for each binding, `pass`, `fail` or `not-applicable` and then the
    /// capability, the list, the bound path and, for a pass, the counts of its
    /// proof, for a failure the reason on the same line (the build or the test
    /// failed, it ran past its limit, or it proves nothing: zero tests, an
    /// `#[ignore]`d test, a path that does not resolve or does not match its
    /// pattern). A reason too long for a line keeps its start and its end. It
    /// prints nothing for a run with no binding. The report is a
    /// value: nothing was written anywhere.
    #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    pub struct VerifyReport {
        change: Option<String>,
        evidence: Vec<Evidence>,
    }
    
    impl VerifyReport {
        /// The slug of the change that was verified, or `None` for a run over
        /// every capability.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn change(&self) -> Option<&str> {
            self.change.as_deref()
        }
    
        /// The evidence of each binding, in order.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn evidence(&self) -> &[Evidence] {
            &self.evidence
        }
    
        /// The number of bindings that ran or were refused.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
            self.evidence.len()
        }
    
        /// Whether there was no binding to run.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
            self.evidence.is_empty()
        }
    
        /// Whether each binding passed or is justified as non-applicable on this host.
        /// True for a run with no binding, since
        /// none failed; the exit code of a command that prints this report is 0
        /// exactly then.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn passed(&self) -> bool {
            self.evidence.iter().all(|evidence| {
                matches!(
                    evidence.outcome(),
                    BoundOutcome::Passed | BoundOutcome::NotApplicable { .. }
                )
            })
        }
    
        /// The bindings whose execution proof passed; excludes non-applicable bindings.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn passed_count(&self) -> usize {
            self.evidence
                .iter()
                .filter(|evidence| evidence.passed())
                .count()
        }
    
        /// The bindings whose known compiler platform guard excludes this host.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn not_applicable_count(&self) -> usize {
            self.evidence
                .iter()
                .filter(|evidence| matches!(evidence.outcome(), BoundOutcome::NotApplicable { .. }))
                .count()
        }
    
        /// The line of each binding, in order, as `Display` prints them: one line
        /// each, with no line ending.
        #[must_use]
        pub fn lines(&self) -> Vec<String> {
            self.evidence.iter().map(evidence_line).collect()
        }
    
        /// The bindings that failed or are unresolved; excludes host non-applicability.
        pub fn failures(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &Evidence> {
            self.evidence.iter().filter(|evidence| {
                !matches!(
                    evidence.outcome(),
                    BoundOutcome::Passed | BoundOutcome::NotApplicable { .. }
                )
            })
        }
    }
    
    impl fmt::Display for VerifyReport {
        fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
            for line in self.lines() {
                writeln!(f, "{line}")?;
            }
            Ok(())
        }
    }
    
    /// The line `verify` prints for one binding: `pass` or `fail`, the capability,
    /// the list, the bound path and then the counts of the proof or the reason.
    pub(super) fn evidence_line(evidence: &Evidence) -> String {
        let list = evidence.kind().key();
        match evidence.outcome() {
            BoundOutcome::NotApplicable { reason } => format!(
                "not-applicable {} {list} `{}`: {}",
                evidence.capability(),
                evidence.path(),
                one_line(reason)
            ),
            BoundOutcome::Passed => format!(
                "pass {} {list} `{}` ({})",
                evidence.capability(),
                evidence.path(),
                evidence.proof()
            ),
            BoundOutcome::Failed { reason } => format!(
                "fail {} {list} `{}`: {}",
                evidence.capability(),
                evidence.path(),
                one_line(reason)
            ),
            BoundOutcome::Unresolved { reason } => format!(
                "fail {} {list} `{}`: unresolved binding: {}",
                evidence.capability(),
                evidence.path(),
                one_line(&reason.to_string())
            ),
        }
    }
    
    /// `text` on one line. A long text keeps its start and its end, which hold the
    /// command and the counts of the run, and drops the middle, which holds the
    /// output of `cargo`.
    fn one_line(text: &str) -> String {
        let joined = text.split_whitespace().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" ");
        let chars: Vec<char> = joined.chars().collect();
        if chars.len() <= REASON {
            return joined;
        }
        let half = REASON / 2;
        let head: String = chars[..half].iter().collect();
        let tail: String = chars[chars.len() - half..].iter().collect();
        format!("{head} ... {tail}")
    }
    
    /// The bindings of the capabilities the change `slug` lists, every one of which
    /// must be loaded.
    fn bindings_of_change(plan: &Workplan<'_>, slug: &str) -> Result<Vec<Binding>, LifecycleError> {
        let impact = plan.impact(slug)?;
        let loaded: BTreeSet<&str> = plan
            .capabilities()
            .iter()
            .map(|cap| cap.value().header.id.as_str())
            .collect();
        for id in &impact.capabilities {
            if !loaded.contains(id.as_str()) {
                return Err(ReconcileError::CapabilityNotLoaded {
                    change: impact.change.clone(),
                    capability: id.clone(),
                    findings: capability_path(id).map_or_default(|path| plan.findings_of(&path)),
                }
                .into());
            }
        }
        Ok(impact.bindings)
    }
    
    /// The bindings of every capability, none of whose documents may be unloaded.
    fn bindings_of_every_capability(plan: &Workplan<'_>) -> Result<Vec<Binding>, LifecycleError> {
        let unloaded: Vec<String> = plan
            .unloaded()
            .into_iter()
            .filter(|id| id.starts_with("cap:"))
            .collect();
        if unloaded.is_empty() {
            return Ok(plan.graph().bindings().to_vec());
        }
        let findings = unloaded
            .iter()
            .filter_map(|id| capability_path(id))
            .flat_map(|path| plan.findings_of(&path))
            .collect();
        Err(LifecycleError::Unloaded {
            capabilities: unloaded,
            findings,
        })
    }
    
    /// Run the bound tests and the bound examples of the change `change`, or of
    /// every capability when `change` is `None`, through `runner`, and report each
    /// binding.
    ///
    /// A test is judged by the proof that exactly one test ran and passed, an
    /// example target by its exit status within the limit, a documentation test by
    /// the `ok` line of each entry the list pass selected (see
    /// [`Executor`]). A binding that does not resolve, is not a `#[test]`, or whose
    /// value fails its pattern (`-Zunstable-options`, `--include-ignored`,
    /// `--exact`, a path with `;` or a space) is reported as unresolved and
    /// never reaches `cargo`. `code` bindings are not run.
    ///
    /// This does not ask `git` anything and does not go through the Git version
    /// gate of [`Lifecycle::open`](super::Lifecycle::open). It writes nothing in
    /// the repository; `cargo` builds into its own target directory.
    ///
    /// For a change, every capability it lists must be loaded. For every
    /// capability, no capability document may be unloaded: what it binds is
    /// unknown, and a run that skipped it would pass.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// [`LifecycleError::Change`] when the slug names no loaded record;
    /// [`LifecycleError::Reconcile`] when a listed capability did not load;
    /// [`LifecycleError::Unloaded`] for a run over every capability while one did
    /// not load; [`LifecycleError::Exec`] when a process cannot be started or
    /// read to its end, which is no verdict.
    pub fn verify<R: Runner + ?Sized>(
        opened: &Opened,
        runner: &R,
        change: Option<&str>,
    ) -> Result<VerifyReport, LifecycleError> {
        let plan = opened.workplan();
        let mut bindings = match change {
            Some(slug) => bindings_of_change(&plan, slug)?,
            None => bindings_of_every_capability(&plan)?,
        };
        bindings.retain(|binding| matches!(binding.kind(), BindingKind::Test | BindingKind::Example));
        bindings.sort_by(|a, b| {
            (a.capability(), a.kind(), a.path()).cmp(&(b.capability(), b.kind(), b.path()))
        });
        let executor = Executor::new(opened.workspace(), runner)?;
        let evidence = bindings
            .iter()
            .map(|binding| executor.run(binding))
            .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?;
        Ok(VerifyReport {
            change: change.map(|slug| crate::lookup::bare_slug(slug).to_string()),
            evidence,
        })
    }

    /// What a change reaches: the readers and the evidence its record puts in play.
    ///
    /// Every list is sorted and without repeats, so equal inputs give equal values
    /// and equal text. The value is a pure function of the loaded documents and
    /// the source index; building it writes nothing. `Display` is the text
    /// `change impact` prints.
    #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    #[non_exhaustive]
    pub struct Impact {
        /// The slug of the change, without the `change:` prefix.
        pub change: String,
        /// The capabilities in the `changes` of the record, sorted by id.
        pub capabilities: Vec<String>,
        /// The sections in the `affects` of the record, by capability and then in
        /// the order a capability holds its sections.
        pub sections: Vec<SectionId>,
        /// The bindings of the listed capabilities with what the source index made
        /// of each, by capability, then code, tests and examples, then by path.
        pub bindings: Vec<Binding>,
        /// The capabilities that depend on a listed capability, by capability and
        /// then by the capability depended on.
        pub dependants: Vec<Dependant>,
        /// The sections that link to an affected section, by affected section and
        /// then by the section that links.
        pub linking: Vec<LinkingSection>,
    }
symbol raf_workplan::lifecycle::Lifecycle::reconcile (named in the section)
  in crates/raf-workplan/src/lifecycle/session.rs
    /// The full obligation list of the change `change`: the static kinds of
        /// [`Workplan::reconcile`](crate::Workplan::reconcile) and the three that
        /// need a process.
        ///
        /// * `section-untouched`: for each section in `affects`, the section at
        ///   the merge base of `HEAD` and the default branch is compared with the
        ///   working tree ([`section_digest`](super::section_digest)); a section
        ///   with the same authored text is owed. `contract_effect: none` leaves
        ///   out `contract`, `usage` and `examples`. A repository with no default
        ///   branch is an error, so is a merge-base file that does not parse.
        /// * `evidence-changed`: each evidence record a claim of a listed
        ///   capability names is read at the same merge base; one that passed
        ///   there and now fails, holds a value over a limit (its own or the merge
        ///   base's), has lost a limit of the merge base, or no longer loads is
        ///   owed at each claim that names it. A record there that does not read
        ///   as an evidence record is an error.
        /// * `example-failing`: the example targets and documentation tests that
        ///   the capabilities of the change bind and that resolve are built and
        ///   run ([`Executor`]); one that fails, runs no test or proves nothing is
        ///   owed. Test bindings are not run here, and the examples of a binding
        ///   that does not resolve are not either (it is `binding-missing`).
        ///
        /// The merge base is asked for once, and only when a section or a claim
        /// needs it. The documents are loaded as they are on disk at the call.
        /// Nothing in the repository is written.
        ///
        /// # Errors
        ///
        /// [`LifecycleError::Change`] and [`LifecycleError::Reconcile`] as for
        /// [`Workplan::reconcile`](crate::Workplan::reconcile);
        /// [`LifecycleError::Git`] and [`LifecycleError::Document`] when a section
        /// or a record cannot be compared; [`LifecycleError::Exec`] when a process
        /// of an example cannot be started or read to its end.
        pub fn reconcile(&self, change: &str) -> Result<Reconciled, LifecycleError> {
            let plan = self.opened.workplan();
            let fixed = plan.reconcile(change)?;
            let impact = plan.impact(change)?;
            let effect = plan.change(change)?.value().contract_effect;
            let mut owed = fixed.obligations().to_vec();
            owed.extend(self.process_obligations(
                &self.opened,
                &plan,
                &impact,
                effect,
                true,
                &mut None,
            )?);
            Ok(Reconciled::new(fixed.change().to_string(), owed))
        }
